## May 7th / 5月7日

===Original===
I love my garden. I am writing in it now in the late afternoon loveliness, much interrupted by the mosquitoes and the temptation to look at all the glories of the new green leaves washed half an hour ago in a cold shower. Two owls are perched near me, and are carrying on a long conversation that I enjoy as much as any warbling of nightingales. The gentleman owl says , and she answers from her tree a little way off, , beautifully assenting to and completing her lord’s remark, as becomes a properly constructed German she-owl. They say the same thing over and over again so emphatically that I think it must be something nasty about me; but I shall not let myself be frightened away by the sarcasm of owls.

This is less a garden than a wilderness. No one has lived in the house, much less in the garden, for twenty-five years, and it is such a pretty old place that the people who might have lived here and did not, deliberately preferring the horrors of a flat in a town, must have belonged to that vast number of eyeless and earless persons of whom the world seems chiefly composed. Noseless too, though it does not sound pretty; but the greater part of my spring happiness is due to the scent of the wet earth and young leaves.

I am always happy (out of doors be it understood, for indoors there are servants and furniture) but in quite different ways, and my spring happiness bears no resemblance to my summer or autumn happiness, though it is not more intense, and there were days last winter when I danced for sheer joy out in my frost-bound garden, in spite of my years and children. But I did it behind a bush, having a due regard for the decencies.

===Chinese===
我爱我的花园。此刻我正坐在园中写作，正值黄昏最可爱的时候，不时被蚊子打断，也忍不住要抬眼去看那些新绿叶片的万般荣光——半小时前一场冷雨刚把它们洗过一遍。两只猫头鹰栖在我近旁，正进行着一场冗长的谈话，我听得津津有味，不亚于听夜莺啭鸣。雄猫头鹰说着「」，她从不远处自己的树上应道「」，婉转地应和着、补足她夫君的话，恰如一只构造得体的德国雌猫头鹰所当为。他们把同一句话翻来覆去说得那么起劲，我想那必是关于我的什么坏话；但我可不会让猫头鹰的讥讽刺得逃走。

与其说这是个花园，不如说是一片荒野。二十五年没有人住过这房子，更没有人打理过这园子，而它是这样一处可爱的老宅，那些本可以住在这里却没有住、偏要选择城里公寓种种可怕的人，想必属于那为数众多的无眼无耳之人——这世界似乎主要就是由这类人组成的。还有无鼻的，虽然说出来不甚好听；但我春天的快乐，大半都得归功于湿土和嫩叶的芬芳。

我总是快乐的（当然是指在户外，因为屋里有仆人和家具），但快乐的方式各不相同；我春天的快乐与夏天、秋天的快乐毫无相似之处，虽然也并不更强烈。去年冬天就有那么几天，我不顾自己的年纪和孩子，在冻得硬邦邦的花园里纯粹出于欢喜跳起舞来。不过我是躲在灌木丛后面跳的，对体面还是有着应有的顾忌。

===Original===
There are so many bird-cherries round me, great trees with branches sweeping the grass, and they are so wreathed just now with white blossoms and tenderest green that the garden looks like a wedding. I never saw such masses of them; they seemed to fill the place. Even across a little stream that bounds the garden on the east, and right in the middle of the cornfield beyond, there is an immense one, a picture of grace and glory against the cold blue of the spring sky.

My garden is surrounded by cornfields and meadows, and beyond are great stretches of sandy heath and pine forests, and where the forests leave off the bare heath begins again; but the forests are beautiful in their lofty, pink-stemmed vastness, far overhead the crowns of softest gray-green, and underfoot a bright green wortleberry carpet, and everywhere the breathless silence; and the bare heaths are beautiful too, for one can see across them into eternity almost, and to go out on to them with one’s face towards the setting sun is like going into the very presence of God.

===Chinese===
我周围有这么多稠李，一棵棵大树，枝条拂着草面，此刻缀满白花和最柔嫩的绿，整个花园看起来像一场婚礼。我从没见过这么一大片一大片的；它们仿佛把此地都填满了。就连东面界住花园的小溪对岸，再往远处麦田的正中央，还立着一棵高大的，映着春日冷蓝的天空，俨然一幅典雅而辉煌的图画。

我的花园四周是麦田和草地，再往外是大片大片的沙质荒原和松林，松林尽头又是光秃秃的荒原；但这些森林以其高峻、粉红树干的恢弘而美丽，头顶高处是极其柔和的灰绿色树冠，脚下是亮绿色的越橘地毯，四处是屏息般的寂静；光秃的荒原也美，因为人几乎可以望穿它们直达永恒，走出去面朝落日，就像走到了上帝的面前。

===Original===
In the middle of this plain is the oasis of bird-cherries and greenery where I spend my happy days, and in the middle of the oasis is the gray stone house with many gables where I pass my reluctant nights. The house is very old, and has been added to at various times. It was a convent before the Thirty Years’ War, and the vaulted chapel, with its brick floor worn by pious peasant knees, is now used as a hall. Gustavus Adolphus and his Swedes passed through more than once, as is duly recorded in archives still preserved, for we are on what was then the high road between Sweden and Brandenburg the unfortunate. The Lion of the North was no doubt an estimable person and acted wholly up to his convictions, but he must have sadly upset the peaceful nuns, who were not without convictions of their own, sending them out on to the wide, empty plain to piteously seek some life to replace the life of silence here.

===Chinese===
在这片平原的中央，是稠李与绿意的绿洲，我在这里度过我快乐的日子；绿洲中央，是有许多山墙的灰色石屋，我在那里度过我不情愿的夜晚。这房子很老了，历代屡有增建。三十年战争之前它是一座修道院，那拱顶小教堂，砖砌的地面已被虔诚农人的膝盖磨出凹痕，如今用作大厅。古斯塔夫·阿道夫和他的瑞典人不只一次路过此地，至今保存的档案里有确凿的记载，因为我们正处在当年连接瑞典与不幸的勃兰登堡的大道上。北方雄狮无疑是个可敬的人，行事完全遵照自己的信念，但他想必大大搅扰了那些安分的修女——她们也并非没有自己的信念——把她们打发到空旷的大平原上，凄凄惶惶去寻一种生活，来顶替这里静默的生涯。

===Original===
From nearly all the windows of the house I can look out across the plain, with no obstacle in the shape of a hill, right away to a blue line of distant forest, and on the west side uninterruptedly to the setting sun﻿—nothing but a green, rolling plain, with a sharp edge against the sunset. I love those west windows better than any others, and have chosen my bedroom on that side of the house so that even times of hair-brushing may not be entirely lost, and the young woman who attends to such matters has been taught to fulfil her duties about a mistress recumbent in an easy-chair before an open window, and not to profane with chatter that sweet and solemn time. This girl is grieved at my habit of living almost in the garden, and all her ideas as to the sort of life a respectable German lady should lead have got into a sad muddle since she came to me. The people round about are persuaded that I am, to put it as kindly as possible, exceedingly eccentric, for the news has travelled that I spend the day out of doors with a book, and that no mortal eye has ever yet seen me sew or cook. But why cook when you can get someone to cook for you? And as for sewing, the maids will hem the sheets better and quicker than I could, and all forms of needlework of the fancy order are inventions of the evil one for keeping the foolish from applying their heart to wisdom.

===Chinese===
从房子几乎所有的窗户望出去，我都能越过平原，没有任何山丘之类的遮挡，一直望到远处森林的蓝色轮廓线，在西边则可以毫无遮拦地望到落日——只有一片起伏的绿色平原，衬着落日，边缘分明。我最爱那些西窗，胜过其他所有的窗，特意把卧室选在房子这一侧，这样连梳头的时光也不至于全然虚掷；那位照料此类事务的年轻姑娘已经受了调教，懂得该在女主人临窗斜倚安乐椅时履行她的职责，而不得用闲话亵渎那甜美庄严的时刻。这姑娘为我几乎住在花园里的习惯深感痛心，自从来我这里，她对一位体面的德国太太该过怎样的生活的全部见解，都搅成了一团糟。周围的人则确信，说得尽量客气些，我古怪至极，因为消息已经传开，说我整天带着一本书待在户外，还说我这双肉眼凡胎从未被看见做过针线或下过厨房。可是既然能雇人替你做饭，为什么还要自己做呢？至于针线，女仆们缝床单总比我缝得又快又好；而一切花哨的绣活，都是恶魔的发明，好让蠢人不把心思用在智慧上。

===Original===
We had been married five years before it struck us that we might as well make use of this place by coming down and living in it. Those five years were spent in a flat in a town, and during their whole interminable length I was perfectly miserable and perfectly healthy, which disposes of the ugly notion that has at times disturbed me that my happiness here is less due to the garden than to a good digestion. And while we were wasting our lives there, here was this dear place with dandelions up to the very door, all the paths grass-grown and completely effaced, in winter so lonely, with nobody but the north wind taking the least notice of it, and in May﻿—in all those five lovely Mays﻿—no one to look at the wonderful bird-cherries and still more wonderful masses of lilacs, everything glowing and blowing, the Virginia creeper madder every year, until at last, in October, the very roof was wreathed with bloodred tresses, the owls and the squirrels and all the blessed little birds reigning supreme, and not a living creature ever entering the empty house except the snakes, which got into the habit during those silent years of wriggling up the south wall into the rooms on that side whenever the old housekeeper opened the windows. All that was here﻿—peace, and happiness, and a reasonable life﻿—and yet it never struck me to come and live in it. Looking back I am astonished, and can in no way account for the tardiness of my discovery that here, in this faraway corner, was my kingdom of heaven. Indeed, so little did it enter my head to even use the place in summer, that I submitted to weeks of seaside life with all its horrors every year; until at last, in the early spring of last year, having come down for the opening of the village school, and wandering out afterwards into the bare and desolate garden, I don’t know what smell of wet earth or rotting leaves brought back my childhood with a rush and all the happy days I had spent in a garden. Shall I ever forget that day? It was the beginning of my real life, my coming of age as it were, and entering into my kingdom. Early March, gray, quiet skies, and brown, quiet earth; leafless and sad and lonely enough out there in the damp and silence, yet there I stood feeling the same rapture of pure delight in the first breath of spring that I used to as a child, and the five wasted years fell from me like a cloak, and the world was full of hope, and I vowed myself then and there to nature, and have been happy ever since.

===Chinese===
我们结婚五年之后，才忽然想到不妨利用一下这个地方，搬下来住。那五年是在城里一套公寓里度过的，在那漫长得没有尽头的岁月里，我彻底地痛苦着，又彻底地健康着——这就驳倒了一个不时来搅扰我的难看念头：我在此地的幸福，与其说归功于花园，不如说归功于一副好肠胃。当我们在那边虚掷光阴的时候，这个可爱的地方却在这里：蒲公英一直长到门口，所有小径都长满荒草、彻底湮没了，冬天那样孤寂，除了北风没有谁肯对它稍加理会；而到了五月——在那五个可爱的五月里——没有人来看美妙的稠李，看更美妙的成片成片的丁香，万物灼灼盛放，五叶地锦一年红似一年，直到最后，十月里，连屋顶都缠满了血红色的发绺；猫头鹰、松鼠和一切蒙福的小鸟称王称霸，除了蛇，再没有活物进过那空屋——那些静默的年头里，蛇养成了习惯，每逢老管家婆开窗，就从南墙爬进那一侧的房间。这里本有一切——安宁、幸福、一种合乎情理的生活——而我竟然从未想到要来住。回想起来我很惊讶，怎么也无法解释，我何以迟迟才发现：就在这个遥远的角落里，有着我的天国。真的，我脑子里连夏天来用一用这地方都没想到过，以至于年年都忍受着海滨度假数周的种种可怕；直到去年早春，我下来参加村校的落成典礼，随后信步走进那光秃秃、满目荒凉的花园——不知是湿土还是腐叶的什么气味，猛地唤回了我的童年，和我在花园里度过的所有快乐日子。我怎会忘记那一天呢？那是我真正生活的开端，可以说是我的成年礼，是我进入了自己的王国。三月初，灰色而安静的天空，棕色而安静的大地；在潮气和寂静中，那里光秃秃的，够凄凉也够孤单，可我就站在那儿，在春天的第一缕气息里，感到儿时那种纯粹欢喜的狂喜；那五年虚度的岁月像一件斗篷从我身上滑落，世界充满希望，我当场就把自己许给了自然，从此一直快乐着。

===Original===
My other half being indulgent, and with some faint thought perhaps that it might be as well to look after the place, consented to live in it at any rate for a time; whereupon followed six specially blissful weeks from the end of April into June, during which I was here alone, supposed to be superintending the painting and papering, but as a matter of fact only going into the house when the workmen had gone out of it.

How happy I was! I don’t remember any time quite so perfect since the days when I was too little to do lessons and was turned out with sugar on my eleven o’clock bread and butter on to a lawn closely strewn with dandelions and daisies. The sugar on the bread and butter has lost its charm, but I love the dandelions and daisies even more passionately now than then, and never would endure to see them all mown away if I were not certain that in a day or two they would be pushing up their little faces again as jauntily as ever. During those six weeks I lived in a world of dandelions and delights. The dandelions carpeted the three lawns﻿—they used to be lawns, but have long since blossomed out into meadows filled with every sort of pretty weed﻿—and under and among the groups of leafless oaks and beeches were blue hepaticas, white anemones, violets, and celandines in sheets. The celandines in particular delighted me with their clean, happy brightness, so beautifully trim and newly varnished, as though they too had had the painters at work on them. Then, when the anemones went, came a few stray periwinkles and Solomon’s Seal, and all the bird-cherries blossomed in a burst. And then, before I had a little got used to the joy of their flowers against the sky, came the lilacs﻿—masses and masses of them, in clumps on the grass, with other shrubs and trees by the side of walks, and one great continuous bank of them half a mile long right past the west front of the house, away down as far as one could see, shining glorious against a background of firs. When that time came, and when, before it was over, the acacias all blossomed too, and four great clumps of pale, silvery-pink peonies flowered under the south windows, I felt so absolutely happy, and blest, and thankful, and grateful, that I really cannot describe it. My days seemed to melt away in a dream of pink and purple peace.

===Chinese===
我的另一半倒是纵容的，或许还隐隐觉得照看照看这地方也不错，便同意住进来，至少住上一阵子；于是从四月末到六月，接续着六个格外极乐的星期，其间我独自在此，名义上是监督油漆和糊壁纸的工程，实际上只有等工人们出去了，我才进那房子。

我多么快乐啊！自从那些日子——我那时太小，还做不了功课，十一点钟的黄油面包上撒了糖，就被打发到密密铺满蒲公英和雏菊的草坪上——以后，我不记得还有过如此完美的时光。面包上的糖已失去了它的魅力，可我对蒲公英和雏菊的热爱，如今比那时还要炽烈；若不是确信一两天后它们又会像往常一样神气活现地探出小脸来，我绝不忍看它们被尽数割去。那六个星期里，我生活在一个蒲公英与欢乐的世界里。三块草坪上铺满了蒲公英——它们本是草坪，但早已开成了一片片长满各种漂亮野花的草甸——在光秃的栎树和山毛榉的树丛下、树丛间，是大片大片的蓝色獐耳细辛、白色银莲花、堇菜和白屈菜。白屈菜尤其让我欢喜，它们洁净、快活的亮色，收拾得那样精致，像刚上过漆，仿佛油漆匠也在它们身上忙活过。随后，银莲花谢去，来了几株散落的蔓长春花和玉竹，所有的稠李一齐绽放。然后，我还没怎么习惯它们的花朵映着天空的喜悦，丁香就来了——一丛又一丛，一团团立在草地上，还有别的灌木和树沿着小径旁边，更有一长条连绵的丁香花堤，半英里长，恰好经过房子的西正面，一直延伸到目力尽头，映着冷杉的背景，闪耀着辉煌。当那时节来到，当它还未过去、刺槐也全都开了花，四大丛淡银粉色的芍药在南窗下绽放时，我感到那样彻底的幸福、蒙福、感谢、感激，实在无法形容。我的日子仿佛融化在粉与紫的安宁之梦里。

===Original===
There were only the old housekeeper and her handmaiden in the house, so that on the plea of not giving too much trouble I could indulge what my other half calls my *fantaisie déréglée* as regards meals﻿—that is to say, meals so simple that they could be brought out to the lilacs on a tray; and I lived, I remember, on salad and bread and tea the whole time, sometimes a very tiny pigeon appearing at lunch to save me, as the old lady thought, from starvation. Who but a woman could have stood salad for six weeks, even salad sanctified by the presence and scent of the most gorgeous lilac masses? I did, and grew in grace every day, though I have never liked it since. How often now, oppressed by the necessity of assisting at three dining-room meals daily, two of which are conducted by the functionaries held indispensable to a proper maintenance of the family dignity, and all of which are pervaded by joints of meat, how often do I think of my salad days, forty in number, and of the blessedness of being alone as I was then alone!

===Chinese===
房子里只有老管家婆和她的使女，于是借口不想太麻烦人，我在伙食上便可放纵我的另一半所谓的 *fantaisie déréglée*（不着调的怪念头）——也就是说，饭菜简单到可以用托盘端到丁香花下去吃；我记得那段时间我全靠沙拉、面包和茶过活，偶尔午餐时会出现一只很小很小的鸽子，好依老太太的想法，把我从饿死的边缘救回来。除了女人，谁能连吃六个星期的沙拉——哪怕是被最壮丽的丁香花丛的身影与芬芳祝圣过的沙拉？我就做到了，而且恩典日增，尽管从那以后我再没喜欢过它。如今，每当被每天三餐都要亲临饭厅的义务压得喘不过气——其中两餐还得由那些对于妥善维持家庭尊严不可或缺的执事们主持，而且餐餐都弥漫着大块大块的肉——我多么常常想起我那四十个沙拉的日子，想起像当时那样独自一人的至福啊！

===Original===
And then the evenings, when the workmen had all gone and the house was left to emptiness and echoes, and the old housekeeper had gathered up her rheumatic limbs into her bed, and my little room in quite another part of the house had been set ready, how reluctantly I used to leave the friendly frogs and owls, and with my heart somewhere down in my shoes lock the door to the garden behind me, and pass through the long series of echoing south rooms full of shadows and ladders and ghostly pails of painters’ mess, and humming a tune to make myself believe I liked it, go rather slowly across the brick-floored hall, up the creaking stairs, down the long whitewashed passage, and with a final rush of panic whisk into my room and double lock and bolt the door!

There were no bells in the house, and I used to take a great dinner-bell to bed with me so that at least I might be able to make a noise if frightened in the night, though what good it would have been I don’t know, as there was no one to hear. The housemaid slept in another little cell opening out of mine, and we two were the only living creatures in the great empty west wing. She evidently did not believe in ghosts, for I could hear how she fell asleep immediately after getting into bed; nor do I believe in them, “*mais je les redoute*,” as a French lady said, who from her books appears to have been strong-minded.

===Chinese===
还有那些夜晚，等工人们全都走了，房子只剩下空寂和回声，老管家婆把她风湿的手脚收拢到床上，我那间位于房子另一头的小屋也收拾停当，我总是那样不情愿地离开友善的青蛙和猫头鹰，心一直沉到鞋底，在身后锁上通花园的门，穿过一长串回声阵阵的南屋——满屋是影子、梯子和幽灵似的一桶桶油漆脏水——哼着曲子好让自己相信我喜欢这一切，脚步相当缓慢地走过砖地大厅，登上吱呀作响的楼梯，穿过长长的粉刷过道，最后带着一阵恐慌冲进我的房间，双重上锁，还插上门闩！

房子里没有铃，我惯于带一只大餐铃上床，这样夜里若是害怕，至少还能弄出点声响，虽然那有什么用我也不知道，因为没人听得见。女仆睡在从我房间开出的一间小单间里，我们两个是空荡荡的西翼仅有的活物。她显然不信鬼，因为我听得出她一上床就睡着了；我也不信鬼，「*mais je les redoute*」（但我怕它们），一位法国女士这样说，从她的著作看来，她该是个意志坚强的女人。

===Original===
The dinner-bell was a great solace; it was never rung, but it comforted me to see it on the chair beside my bed, as my nights were anything but placid, it was all so strange, and there were such queer creakings and other noises. I used to lie awake for hours, startled out of a light sleep by the cracking of some board, and listen to the indifferent snores of the girl in the next room. In the morning, of course, I was as brave as a lion and much amused at the cold perspirations of the night before; but even the nights seem to me now to have been delightful, and myself like those historic boys who heard a voice in every wind and snatched a fearful joy. I would gladly shiver through them all over again for the sake of the beautiful purity of the house, empty of servants and upholstery.

How pretty the bedrooms looked with nothing in them but their cheerful new papers! Sometimes I would go into those that were finished and build all sorts of castles in the air about their future and their past. Would the nuns who had lived in them know their little whitewashed cells again, all gay with delicate flower papers and clean white paint? And how astonished they would be to see cell No. 14 turned into a bathroom, with a bath big enough to insure a cleanliness of body equal to their purity of soul! They would look upon it as a snare of the tempter; and I know that in my own case I only began to be shocked at the blackness of my nails the day that I began to lose the first whiteness of my soul by falling in love at fifteen with the parish organist, or rather with the glimpse of surplice and Roman nose and fiery moustache which was all I ever saw of him, and which I loved to distraction for at least six months; at the end of which time, going out with my governess one day, I passed him in the street, and discovered that his unofficial garb was a frock-coat combined with a turndown collar and a “bowler” hat, and never loved him any more.

===Chinese===
那只餐铃是极大的安慰；它从没响过，可看见它放在床边的椅子上，我便感到心安，因为我的夜晚实在说不上平静，一切都那么陌生，还有种种古怪的吱嘎声和别的响动。我常常一连几个小时睡不着，被某块木板的爆裂声从浅睡中惊醒，听着隔壁房间里姑娘那无动于衷的鼾声。到了早晨，我当然勇猛如狮子，还笑话前一夜出的冷汗；可如今连那些夜晚在我记忆里也是美妙的，我自己就像那些史书上的男孩，在每一阵风里都听见人语，攫取着一份战栗的欢乐。为了那房子美丽的纯净——没有仆人、没有家具陈设——我甘愿再把那些寒战重新抖一遍。

卧室里什么都不放、只糊着明快的新墙纸，看上去多漂亮啊！有时我会走进那些完工的房间，就它们的过去和未来筑起种种空中楼阁。当年住过这些房间的修女们，还认得她们那些刷白的小单间吗——如今糊着雅致的花卉墙纸，漆着干净的白漆？她们看到14号小室改成了浴室，里面那只浴缸大得足以保证肉体的洁净堪与灵魂的纯洁相配，又会多么惊讶！她们会把这看作诱惑者的陷阱；而我知道，就我自己而言，直到十五岁那年爱上教区风琴师，我才开始为自己指甲的黑而震惊，同时也开始失去灵魂最初的洁白——其实我爱的不过是那白法衣、罗马鼻和火红小胡子的一瞥，那是我见过的他的全部，我为他神魂颠倒了至少六个月；六个月期满，有一天我和家庭教师出门，在街上与他擦肩而过，发现他不着圣职服装时的打扮是长礼服配翻领和圆顶硬礼帽，从此就再也不爱他了。

===Original===
The first part of that time of blessedness was the most perfect, for I had not a thought of anything but the peace and beauty all round me. Then he appeared suddenly who has a right to appear when and how he will and rebuked me for never having written, and when I told him that I had been literally too happy to think of writing, he seemed to take it as a reflection on himself that I could be happy alone. I took him round the garden along the new paths I had had made, and showed him the acacia and lilac glories, and he said that it was the purest selfishness to enjoy myself when neither he nor the offspring were with me, and that the lilacs wanted thoroughly pruning. I tried to appease him by offering him the whole of my salad and toast supper which stood ready at the foot of the little verandah steps when we came back, but nothing appeased that Man of Wrath, and he said he would go straight back to the neglected family. So he went; and the remainder of the precious time was disturbed by twinges of conscience (to which I am much subject) whenever I found myself wanting to jump for joy. I went to look at the painters every time my feet were for taking me to look at the garden; I trotted diligently up and down the passages; I criticised and suggested and commanded more in one day than I had done in all the rest of the time; I wrote regularly and sent my love; but I could not manage to fret and yearn. What are you to do if your conscience is clear and your liver in order and the sun is shining?

===Chinese===
那段蒙福时光的开头最是完美，因为我心里除了四周的安宁与美，再没有别的念头。随后他突然出现了——他想何时、以何种方式出现，自有他的权利——他责备我从没写过信，当我告诉他，我实在是快乐得顾不上想写信，他似乎把能独自快乐这件事看成了对他的冒犯。我领他沿我新开出的小径在园中转了一圈，给他看刺槐和丁香的胜景，他却说，趁他和孩子们都不在时独自享乐，是最纯粹的自私，还说那些丁香亟需彻底修剪。我试着用我的沙拉和烤面包晚餐平息他——我们回来时，那晚餐正摆在游廊小台阶下候着——但什么也平息不了那位愤怒之人，他说他要径直回到被冷落的家人身边去。于是他走了；而那段宝贵时光的余下部分，就不断被良心的刺痛搅扰（我极易受良心刺痛），每当我发现自己想欢喜得跳起来的时候。每当我的脚要带我去花园，我就去看油漆匠；我勤勉地在过道里小跑往返；我一天里挑剔、建议、下令的次数比先前全部时间加起来还多；我定期写信，捎去我的爱；可我怎么也愁不起来、思念不起来。倘若你良心清白、肝脏正常、阳光又正好，你还能怎么办呢？

## May 10th / 5月10日

===Original===
I knew nothing whatever last year about gardening and this year know very little more, but I have dawnings of what may be done, and have at least made one great stride﻿—from ipomoea to tea-roses.

The garden was an absolute wilderness. It is all round the house, but the principal part is on the south side and has evidently always been so. The south front is one-storied, a long series of rooms opening one into the other, and the walls are covered with Virginia creeper. There is a little verandah in the middle, leading by a flight of rickety wooden steps down into what seems to have been the only spot in the whole place that was ever cared for. This is a semicircle cut into the lawn and edged with privet, and in this semicircle are eleven beds of different sizes bordered with box and arranged round a sundial, and the sundial is very venerable and moss-grown, and greatly beloved by me. These beds were the only sign of any attempt at gardening to be seen (except a solitary crocus that came up all by itself each spring in the grass, not because it wanted to, but because it could not help it), and these I had sown with ipomoea, the whole eleven, having found a German gardening book, according to which ipomoea in vast quantities was the one thing needful to turn the most hideous desert into a paradise. Nothing else in that book was recommended with anything like the same warmth, and being entirely ignorant of the quantity of seed necessary, I bought ten pounds of it and had it sown not only in the eleven beds but round nearly every tree, and then waited in great agitation for the promised paradise to appear. It did not, and I learned my first lesson.

===Chinese===
去年我对园艺还一无所知，今年也强不了多少，不过对于能做些什么，我已隐约瞥见了几线曙光，而且至少迈出了一大步——从牵牛花（ipomoea）到茶香月季（tea-rose）。

这座花园原是一片彻底的荒野。它环绕着屋子四面，但主要部分在南侧，而且显然一向如此。南侧的正屋只有一层，一长列房间彼此相通，墙上爬满了五叶地锦（Virginia creeper）。正中有一座小小的游廊，顺着一道摇摇欲坠的木梯下去，通到一块地方——这似乎是整座庄园里唯一曾被人经心料理过的角落。那是草坪上辟出的一块半圆，镶着女贞的边；半圆里共有十一张花床，大小不一，围着黄杨镶边，环着一座日晷排开。这座日晷古老得很，生满了苔藓，是我心头极爱之物。这些花床是全园仅见的园艺尝试的痕迹（除了每年春天独自在草丛里冒头的一株孤零零的番红花——它并非情愿，只是情不自禁），而我在这些床里播的是牵牛花，十一张床全播了，因为我寻到了一本德文园艺书，照那书上的说法，要把最不堪入目的荒漠变成乐园，只消铺天盖地种下牵牛花便万事俱足。那本书里再没有别的什么东西受过如此热忱的推荐，而我全然不知需要多少种子，竟买了十磅，不但播满了十一张床，还撒遍了几乎每一棵树的四周，然后提心吊胆地等着那应许的乐园降临。它没有来，于是我学到了第一课。

===Original===
Luckily I had sown two great patches of sweet-peas which made me very happy all the summer, and then there were some sunflowers and a few hollyhocks under the south windows, with Madonna lilies in between. But the lilies, after being transplanted, disappeared to my great dismay, for how was I to know it was the way of lilies? And the hollyhocks turned out to be rather ugly colours, so that my first summer was decorated and beautified solely by sweet-peas. At present we are only just beginning to breathe after the bustle of getting new beds and borders and paths made in time for this summer. The eleven beds round the sundial are filled with roses, but I see already that I have made mistakes with some. As I have not a living soul with whom to hold communion on this or indeed on any matter, my only way of learning is by making mistakes. All eleven were to have been carpeted with purple pansies, but finding that I had not enough and that nobody had any to sell me, only six have got their pansies, the others being sown with dwarf mignonette. Two of the eleven are filled with Marie van Houtte roses, two with Viscountess Folkestone, two with Laurette Messimy, one with Souvenir de la Malmaison, one with Adam and Devoniensis, two with Persian Yellow and Bicolor, and one big bed behind the sundial with three sorts of red roses (seventy-two in all), Duke of Teck, Cheshunt Scarlet, and Prefet de Limburg. This bed is, I am sure, a mistake, and several of the others are, I think, but of course I must wait and see, being such an ignorant person. Then I have had two long beds made in the grass on either side of the semicircle, each sown with mignonette, and one filled with Marie van Houtte, and the other with Jules Finger and the Bride; and in a warm corner under the drawing-room windows is a bed of Madame Lambard, Madame de Watteville, and Comtesse Riza du Parc; while farther down the garden, sheltered on the north and west by a group of beeches and lilacs, is another large bed, containing Rubens, Madame Joseph Schwartz, and the Hon. Edith Gifford. All these roses are dwarf; I have only two standards in the whole garden, two Madame George Bruants, and they look like broomsticks. How I long for the day when the tea-roses open their buds! Never did I look forward so intensely to anything; and every day I go the rounds, admiring what the dear little things have achieved in the twenty-four hours in the way of new leaf or increase of lovely red shoot.

===Chinese===
所幸我还播下了两大片香豌豆，它们让我快活了整整一个夏天；此外南窗下还有几株向日葵和几株蜀葵，中间夹着圣母百合。可那些百合移栽之后竟不见了，叫我大为沮丧——我哪里知道百合原是这般脾气？蜀葵开出来的颜色又相当难看，于是我头一个夏天，全凭香豌豆独自装点增色。眼下我们才刚刚喘过气来：为了赶在这个夏天之前把新的花床、花境和小径做好，我们着实忙乱了一场。日晷周围的十一张床都栽满了月季，但我已经看出有几张弄错了。在这件事上——其实在任何事上——我连一个可以促膝相谈的活人都没有，我学习的唯一途径便是犯错。十一张床原本都打算铺满紫色三色堇，可发现数量不够，又没人有得卖我，于是只有六张铺上了三色堇，其余的播了矮生木樨草。十一张床里，两张栽的是 Marie van Houtte 月季，两张 Viscountess Folkestone，两张 Laurette Messimy，一张 Souvenir de la Malmaison，一张 Adam 配 Devoniensis，两张 Persian Yellow 配 Bicolor，日晷后面一张大床栽了三个品种的红月季（总共七十二株）：Duke of Teck、Cheshunt Scarlet 和 Prefet de Limburg。这张床，我敢保是个错误，其余有几张恐怕也是，不过像我这样无知的人，当然只好等着瞧了。此外我又在半圆两侧的草地上开了两张长床，各自播了木樨草，一张填满 Marie van Houtte，另一张栽 Jules Finger 和 the Bride；客厅窗下一个暖和的角落里有一张床，栽着 Madame Lambard、Madame de Watteville 和 Comtesse Riza du Parc；再往花园深处去，北面和西面有一丛山毛榉和丁香挡着风，那里还有一张大床，种着 Rubens、Madame Joseph Schwartz 和 the Hon. Edith Gifford。这些月季全是矮生的；全园里我只有两株独干月季，两株 Madame George Bruant，看上去活像两根扫帚柄。我多么盼望茶香月季打开花苞的那一天！我从未如此热切地期盼过任何事物；每天我都要巡视一周，赞叹那些可爱的小东西在二十四小时里又立下了怎样的功绩——添了新叶，或是又抽出多少可爱的红芽。

===Original===
The hollyhocks and lilies (now flourishing) are still under the south windows in a narrow border on the top of a grass slope, at the foot of which I have sown two long borders of sweet-peas facing the rose beds, so that my roses may have something almost as sweet as themselves to look at until the autumn, when everything is to make place for more tea-roses. The path leading away from this semicircle down the garden is bordered with China roses, white and pink, with here and there a Persian Yellow. I wish now I had put tea-roses there, and I have misgivings as to the effect of the Persian Yellows among the Chinas, for the Chinas are such wee little baby things, and the Persian Yellows look as though they intended to be big bushes.

There is not a creature in all this part of the world who could in the least understand with what heart-beatings I am looking forward to the flowering of these roses, and not a German gardening book that does not relegate all tea-roses to hothouses, imprisoning them for life, and depriving them forever of the breath of God. It was no doubt because I was so ignorant that I rushed in where Teutonic angels fear to tread and made my tea-roses face a northern winter; but they did face it under fir branches and leaves, and not one has suffered, and they are looking today as happy and as determined to enjoy themselves as any roses, I am sure, in Europe.

===Chinese===
蜀葵和百合（如今长势正旺）仍在南窗下一条窄窄的花境里，位居一道草坡的坡顶；坡脚我播了两长溜香豌豆，正对着月季床，好让我的月季们面对着几乎同自己一样香甜的东西，一直看到秋天——到那时，一切都要让位给更多的茶香月季。从这半圆通往花园深处的小径两侧栽着中国月季，白的和粉的，间或夹着一株 Persian Yellow。我现在倒希望当初在那里种的是茶香月季，而且心里直犯嘀咕，Persian Yellow 夹在中国月季中间会是怎样的效果，因为中国月季是那么娇小的婴孩似的东西，而 Persian Yellow 瞧那架势，是打定主意要长成大丛的。

在这一带地方，没有一个活人能稍稍体会我盼着这些月季开花时是怎样的心潮起伏；也没有一本德国园艺书不把茶香月季统统打发进温室，判它们终身监禁，永远剥夺它们呼吸上帝气息的权利。无疑正是因为我无知无畏，才闯进了条顿天使都不敢踏足的禁地，让我的茶香月季直面北方的严冬；但它们在冷杉枝和落叶底下挺了过来，一株也没有受害，今天它们看上去那么快活、那么打定了主意要自得其乐，我敢说，全欧洲的月季没有一株比得上。

## May 14th / 5月14日

===Original===
Today I am writing on the verandah with the three babies, more persistent than mosquitoes, raging round me, and already several of the thirty fingers have been in the ink-pot and the owners consoled when duty pointed to rebukes. But who can rebuke such penitent and drooping sunbonnets? I can see nothing but sunbonnets and pinafores and nimble black legs.

===Chinese===
今天我是在游廊上写作，三个宝贝在我身边闹作一团，比蚊子还难缠，三十根手指已经有好几根伸进过墨水瓶，而当职责示意我训斥时，手指的小主人们早已被哄好了。可谁忍心训斥这样一顶顶悔罪地耷拉着的遮阳帽呢？我满眼只见遮阳帽、小围兜，和一双双灵活的黑腿。

===Original===
These three, their patient nurse, myself, the gardener, and the gardener’s assistant, are the only people who ever go into my garden, but then neither are we ever out of it. The gardener has been here a year and has given me notice regularly on the first of every month, but up to now has been induced to stay on. On the first of this month he came as usual, and with determination written on every feature told me he intended to go in June, and that nothing should alter his decision. I don’t think he knows much about gardening, but he can at least dig and water, and some of the things he sows come up, and some of the plants he plants grow, besides which he is the most unflaggingly industrious person I ever saw, and has the great merit of never appearing to take the faintest interest in what we do in the garden. So I have tried to keep him on, not knowing what the next one may be like, and when I asked him what he had to complain of and he replied “Nothing,” I could only conclude that he has a personal objection to me because of my eccentric preference for plants in groups rather than plants in lines. Perhaps, too, he does not like the extracts from gardening books I read to him sometimes when he is planting or sowing something new. Being so helpless myself, I thought it simpler, instead of explaining, to take the book itself out to him and let him have wisdom at its very source, administering it in doses while he worked. I quite recognise that this must be annoying, and only my anxiety not to lose a whole year through some stupid mistake has given me the courage to do it. I laugh sometimes behind the book at his disgusted face, and wish we could be photographed, so that I may be reminded in twenty years’ time, when the garden is a bower of loveliness and I learned in all its ways, of my first happy struggles and failures.

===Chinese===
进这花园的，只有这三个孩子、她们那位耐心的保姆、我本人、园丁和园丁的助手这几个人，可我们也从不曾离开过这园子。园丁来这里一年了，每月一号都照例向我递一回辞呈，但至今都被劝得留了下来。本月一号他照常来了，满脸都写着决心，告诉我说他六月走，什么也改变不了他的决定。我看他园艺懂得不多，但至少会翻地浇水，他播下的种子有些能出苗，他栽的苗有些能活；何况他是我见过的最不知疲倦的勤快人，还有一大优点：对于我们在这园子里干的事，他从不显得有丝毫兴趣。所以我一直设法留他，因为不知道下一个来的会是什么样的人；我问他有什么不满，他答「没什么」，我只能得出结论：他对我个人有意见，怪我古怪地偏爱成丛的植物，而不是排成直线的植物。也许他还不喜欢我有时在他种新东西时念给他听的园艺书选段。我自己如此无能为力，便觉得与其解释，不如干脆把书拿出去给他，让他从智慧的最源头领受，趁他干活时一剂一剂地喂给他。我完全明白这必定惹人烦，只是生怕因某个愚蠢的失误而赔上整整一年的焦灼，才给了我这样做的勇气。有时我躲在书后笑他那张厌恶的脸，还真希望有人给我们拍张照，好让我在二十年之后——当花园已成一座可爱的绿荫凉亭，而我也谙熟了它的一切门道时——还能想起我最初那些快乐的奋斗与失败。

===Original===
All through April he was putting the perennials we had sown in the autumn into their permanent places, and all through April he went about with a long piece of string making parallel lines down the borders of beautiful exactitude and arranging the poor plants like soldiers at a review. Two long borders were done during my absence one day, and when I explained that I should like the third to have plants in groups and not in lines, and that what I wanted was a natural effect with no bare spaces of earth to be seen, he looked even more gloomily hopeless than usual; and on my going out later on to see the result, I found he had planted two long borders down the sides of a straight walk with little lines of five plants in a row﻿—first five pinks, and next to them five rockets, and behind the rockets five pinks, and behind the pinks five rockets, and so on with different plants of every sort and size down to the end. When I protested, he said he had only carried out my orders and had known it would not look well; so I gave in, and the remaining borders were done after the pattern of the first two, and I will have patience and see how they look this summer, before digging them up again; for it becomes beginners to be humble.

If I could only dig and plant myself! How much easier, besides being so fascinating, to make your own holes exactly where you want them and put in your plants exactly as you choose instead of giving orders that can only be half understood from the moment you depart from the lines laid down by that long piece of string! In the first ecstasy of having a garden all my own, and in my burning impatience to make the waste places blossom like a rose, I did one warm Sunday in last year’s April during the servants’ dinner hour, doubly secure from the gardener by the day and the dinner, slink out with a spade and a rake and feverishly dig a little piece of ground and break it up and sow surreptitious ipomoea, and run back very hot and guilty into the house, and get into a chair and behind a book and look languid just in time to save my reputation. And why not? It is not graceful, and it makes one hot; but it is a blessed sort of work, and if Eve had had a spade in Paradise and known what to do with it, we should not have had all that sad business of the apple.

===Chinese===
整个四月，他都在把我们秋天播下的多年生植物移栽到它们的永久位置上；整个四月，他都拿着一根长绳，在花境里拉出漂亮而精确的平行线，把那些可怜的植物排得像阅兵式上的士兵。有一天我不在，两条长花境种完了；等我解释说我想让第三条按丛来种、不排成行，我要的是自然的效果、看不见裸露的泥土，他的脸色比平常更加阴郁绝望了；随后我出去查看结果，发现他已经沿着一条笔直小径的两边种完了两条长花境，一排排五株一行的小小队列——先是五株石竹，旁边五株香紫罗兰，香紫罗兰后面又是五株石竹，石竹后面又是五株香紫罗兰，如此这般，用各种各样大小不一的植物一直排到尽头。我提出抗议，他说他不过是执行我的命令，而且早知道这样不会好看；我只好让步，余下的花境都照头两条的样式完成了，我要耐着性子看看它们今年夏天长什么样，然后再决定要不要翻掉重种；因为初学者理应谦逊。

要是我能自己挖坑自己栽，那该多好！亲自在恰恰你想要的地方挖出你自己的坑，完全按你的心意把苗放进去，何等省事，又何等迷人——总好过发号施令，而一旦偏离那根长绳定下的路线，命令就只能被听懂一半！在初次拥有完全属于我的花园的狂喜之中，在要让荒芜之地开满玫瑰的炽烈急切之中，去年四月一个温暖的星期天，趁仆人们吃饭的钟点——星期天加上午饭，双重保险让我避开园丁——我带着一把铲子和一把耙子溜出去，狂热地翻了一小块地，把土耙碎，偷偷播下牵牛花种子，然后满身燥热、满怀愧疚地跑回屋里，坐进椅子，拿起一本书挡在面前，及时做出慵懒的样子，保住了我的名声。为什么不呢？这确实不雅观，也让人发热；但这是一种蒙福的劳作，倘若夏娃在乐园里也有一把铲子，并且知道怎么用它，我们就不至于有苹果那桩伤心事了。

===Original===
What a happy woman I am living in a garden, with books, babies, birds, and flowers, and plenty of leisure to enjoy them! Yet my town acquaintances look upon it as imprisonment, and burying, and I don’t know what besides, and would rend the air with their shrieks if condemned to such a life. Sometimes I feel as if I were blest above all my fellows in being able to find my happiness so easily. I believe I should always be good if the sun always shone, and could enjoy myself very well in Siberia on a fine day. And what can life in town offer in the way of pleasure to equal the delight of any one of the calm evenings I have had this month sitting alone at the foot of the verandah steps, with the perfume of young larches all about, and the May moon hanging low over the beeches, and the beautiful silence made only more profound in its peace by the croaking of distant frogs and hooting of owls? A cockchafer darting by close to my ear with a loud hum sends a shiver through me, partly of pleasure at the reminder of past summers, and partly of fear lest he should get caught in my hair. The Man of Wrath says they are pernicious creatures and should be killed. I would rather get the killing done at the end of the summer and not crush them out of such a pretty world at the very beginning of all the fun.

===Chinese===
我是个多么幸福的女人啊，住在花园里，有书、有孩子、有鸟、有花，还有大把的闲暇来享受它们！可我城里的熟人们却把这看作监禁，看作活埋，还不知看作什么别的，若是被判过这样的日子，他们会尖叫得撕破天空。有时我觉得，我能这样轻易地找到自己的幸福，真是蒙福胜过所有同类。我相信只要太阳一直照耀，我就会一直善良；在西伯利亚遇上一个好天，我也能过得非常快活。城里的生活又能拿什么乐趣，来比得上我这个月里任何一个安宁的黄昏呢——独自坐在游廊台阶脚下，四周弥漫着幼龄落叶松的芬芳，五月的月亮低悬在山毛榉上方，那美丽的寂静，只因远处的蛙鸣和猫头鹰的啼叫而愈发深邃安详？一只金龟子嗡的一声紧贴我耳边掠过，激起我一阵战栗，一半是欢喜，因为它让我想起过去的夏天；一半是害怕，怕它缠进我的头发。愤怒之人说它们是害虫，应当消灭。我宁愿把这消灭的工程留到夏天结束时再做，也不肯在所有乐趣刚刚开场的时候，把它们从这样一个美丽的世界里碾出去。

===Original===
This has been quite an eventful afternoon. My eldest baby, born in April, is five years old, and the youngest, born in June, is three; so that the discerning will at once be able to guess the age of the remaining middle or May baby. While I was stooping over a group of hollyhocks planted on the top of the only thing in the shape of a hill the garden possesses, the April baby, who had been sitting pensive on a tree stump close by, got up suddenly and began to run aimlessly about, shrieking and wringing her hands with every symptom of terror. I stared, wondering what had come to her; and then I saw that a whole army of young cows, pasturing in a field next to the garden, had got through the hedge and were grazing perilously near my tea-roses and most precious belongings. The nurse and I managed to chase them away, but not before they had trampled down a border of pinks and lilies in the cruellest way, and made great holes in a bed of China roses, and even begun to nibble at a Jackmanni clematis that I am trying to persuade to climb up a tree trunk. The gloomy gardener happened to be ill in bed, and the assistant was at vespers﻿—as Lutheran Germany calls afternoon tea or its equivalent﻿—so the nurse filled up the holes as well as she could with mould, burying the crushed and mangled roses, cheated forever of their hopes of summer glory, and I stood by looking on dejectedly. The June baby, who is two feet square and valiant beyond her size and years, seized a stick much bigger than herself and went after the cows, the cowherd being nowhere to be seen. She planted herself in front of them brandishing her stick, and they stood in a row and stared at her in great astonishment; and she kept them off until one of the men from the farm arrived with a whip, and having found the cowherd sleeping peacefully in the shade, gave him a sound beating. The cowherd is a great hulking young man, much bigger than the man who beat him, but he took his punishment as part of the day’s work and made no remark of any sort. It could not have hurt him much through his leather breeches, and I think he deserved it; but it must be demoralising work for a strong young man with no brains looking after cows. Nobody with less imagination than a poet ought to take it up as a profession.

===Chinese===
这个下午可真多事。我的大女儿四月宝贝五岁了，最小的六月宝贝三岁；于是眼尖的人立刻就能猜出居中的五月宝贝的年纪。我正弯腰俯身在一丛蜀葵前——它们种在花园里唯一一座形似小丘的土坡顶上——一直若有所思地坐在近旁树桩上的四月宝贝忽然站起来，漫无目的地四处乱跑，尖声叫喊，绞着双手，一副惊恐万状的模样。我愣愣地看着，不知她怎么了；随即我看见，在邻田吃草的一整支青年奶牛大军已经钻过树篱，正在我的茶香月季和最珍贵的宝物们旁边危险地啃草。我和保姆好容易把它们轰了出去，但它们已经以最残忍的方式踩倒了一片石竹和百合的花境，在一床中国月季里踩出好些大窟窿，甚至开始啃一株我正竭力劝说去爬树干的铁线莲 Jackmanni。阴郁的园丁碰巧卧病在床，助手去做晚课了——信路德宗的德国人这样称呼午后茶点或其相当之物——于是保姆用泥土尽力把窟窿填上，埋掉了那些被践踏得稀烂的玫瑰，它们永远失去了夏天绽放荣光的希望，我站在一旁沮丧地看着。六月宝贝——她两英尺见方，勇气却远超她的块头和年纪——抓起一根比她本人还粗大的棍子去追那些牛，放牛的牛倌却不见了踪影。她挥舞着棍子往牛群跟前一站，那些牛排成一排，万分惊讶地盯着她；她就这么拦住它们，直到农场的一个男人拿着鞭子赶来，他发现牛倌正在树荫里酣睡，便结结实实揍了他一顿。牛倌是个五大三粗的年轻人，比揍他的人高大得多，但他把这顿惩罚当作当天活计的一部分受了，没吭一声。隔着皮裤子，想必也打不太疼，我认为他活该；只是对一个身强力壮却没有头脑的年轻人来说，看牛这活计想必是磨人心性的。想象力不及诗人的人，都不该以它为业。

===Original===
After the June baby and I had been welcomed back by the other two with as many hugs as though we had been restored to them from great perils, and while we were peacefully drinking tea under a beech tree, I happened to look up into its mazy green, and there, on a branch quite close to my head, sat a little baby owl. I got on the seat and caught it easily, for it could not fly, and how it had reached the branch at all is a mystery. It is a little round ball of gray fluff, with the quaintest, wisest, solemn face. Poor thing! I ought to have let it go, but the temptation to keep it until the Man of Wrath, at present on a journey, has seen it was not to be resisted, as he has often said how much he would like to have a young owl and try and tame it. So I put it into a roomy cage and slung it up on a branch near where it had been sitting, and which cannot be far from its nest and its mother. We had hardly subsided again to our tea when I saw two more balls of fluff on the ground in the long grass and scarcely distinguishable at a little distance from small molehills. These were promptly united to their relation in the cage, and now when the Man of Wrath comes home, not only shall he be welcomed by a wife decked with the orthodox smiles, but by the three little longed-for owls. Only it seems wicked to take them from their mother, and I know that I shall let them go again some day﻿—perhaps the very next time the Man of Wrath goes on a journey. I put a small pot of water in the cage, though they never could have tasted water yet unless they drink the raindrops off the beech leaves. I suppose they get all the liquid they need from the bodies of the mice and other dainties provided for them by their fond parents. But the raindrop idea is prettier.

===Chinese===
等我和六月宝贝回来，另外两个宝贝扑上来抱了我们又抱，仿佛我们是从大难中被抢救回来的一般；随后我们正在一棵山毛榉下安安静静喝茶，我碰巧抬头望进它迷宫般的绿荫，就在离我头顶很近的一根枝上，坐着一只小小的猫头鹰雏鸟。我踩上座椅，轻易就把它捉住了，因为它还不会飞；它究竟是怎么上到那根枝条上去的，是个谜。它是一小团圆圆的灰色绒球，顶着一张最古灵精怪、最聪明、最严肃的小脸。可怜的小东西！我本该放了它，可把它留到正在旅途中的愤怒之人见过之后再放，这诱惑实在难以抗拒，因为他常说，他多么想养一只小猫头鹰，试着把它驯服。于是我把它放进一只宽敞的笼子，挂在离它方才待的枝头不远的一根树枝上，那里想必离它的巢和它的母亲不远。我们刚刚重新安心喝茶，我又看见长草的地上还有两团绒球，隔一小段距离几乎分不清它们和小小的鼹鼠丘。这两只也立刻被送进笼中与它们的亲眷团聚；这样等愤怒之人回家时，迎接他的不但有一位带着合乎规矩的笑容的妻子，还有三只他盼了许久的小猫头鹰。只是把它们从母亲身边夺来似乎作孽，我知道总有一天我会再放它们走——也许就在愤怒之人下一次出门旅行的时候。我在笼里放了一小罐水，虽然它们想必还从未尝过水，除非它们喝过山毛榉叶上的雨珠。我猜它们需要的全部水分，都来自慈爱的双亲供给它们的老鼠和其他美食的躯体。不过，雨珠的念头毕竟更美些。

## May 15th / 5月15日

===Original===
How cruel it was of me to put those poor little owls into a cage even for one night! I cannot forgive myself, and shall never pander to the Man of Wrath’s wishes again. This morning I got up early to see how they were getting on, and I found the door of the cage wide open and no owls to be seen. I thought of course that somebody had stolen them﻿—some boy from the village, or perhaps the chastised cowherd. But looking about I saw one perched high up in the branches of the beech tree, and then to my dismay one lying dead on the ground. The third was nowhere to be seen, and is probably safe in its nest. The parents must have torn at the bars of the cage until by chance they got the door open, and then dragged the little ones out and up into the tree. The one that is dead must have been blown off the branch, as it was a windy night and its neck is broken. There is one happy life less in the garden today through my fault, and it is such a lovely, warm day﻿—just the sort of weather for young soft things to enjoy and grow in. The babies are greatly distressed, and are digging a grave, and preparing funeral wreaths of dandelions.

Just as I had written that I heard sounds of arrival, and running out I breathlessly told the Man of Wrath how nearly I had been able to give him the owls he has so often said he would like to have, and how sorry I was they were gone, and how grievous the death of one, and so on after the voluble manner of women.

===Chinese===
我把那些可怜的小猫头鹰关进笼子，哪怕只关一夜，也是何等残忍！我不能原谅自己，今后再也不会一味迎合愤怒之人的心意了。今天早晨我早早起身去看它们怎样了，却发现笼门大开，猫头鹰已不见了踪影。我自然以为是有人把它们偷走了——也许是村里的哪个男孩，也许是那个挨过训斥的牛倌。可是四下一望，我看见一只高高地栖在山毛榉的枝头，接着又惊愕地发现一只直挺挺躺在地上，死了。第三只哪儿也找不到，多半正安稳地待在窝里。一定是猫头鹰的父母扑啄笼子的木条，直到碰巧把笼门弄开，然后把小家伙们拖出去、带上树去。那只死了的准是被风从枝头刮落的，因为夜里风大，它的脖子折断了。由于我的过错，今天的花园里少了一条快活的小生命，而这又是多么可爱、多么温暖的一天——正是年幼娇嫩的小生灵们享受阳光、茁壮生长的好天气。三个宝贝伤心极了，正在挖一座坟墓，并用蒲公英扎葬礼的花环。

刚写完这句话，我就听见了有人到门的动静，于是跑出去，气喘吁吁地对愤怒之人说，我差一点儿就能把那对猫头鹰送给他了——他不是常念叨着想要吗——它们没了我是多么遗憾，其中一只死了又是何等令人痛心，如此等等，尽是女人们那副滔滔不绝的做派。

===Original===
He listened till I paused to breathe, and then he said, “I am surprised at such cruelty. How could you make the mother owl suffer so? She had never done you any harm.”

Which sent me out of the house and into the garden more convinced than ever that he sang true who sang﻿—

> Two paradises ’twere in one to live in Paradise alone.

===Chinese===
他一直听我讲，直到我停下来喘气，然后他说：「如此残忍，实在出乎我的意料。你怎么能让猫头鹰妈妈这样受苦？她可从来没有伤害过你。」

这话把我气出了屋子、送进了花园，也让我比以往任何时候都更加确信，唱出下面这行诗的人唱得千真万确——

> 独自住在乐园里，一个乐园便抵得上两个乐园。

## May 16th / 5月16日

===Original===
The garden is the place I go to for refuge and shelter, not the house. In the house are duties and annoyances, servants to exhort and admonish, furniture, and meals; but out there blessings crowd round me at every step﻿—it is there that I am sorry for the unkindness in me, for those selfish thoughts that are so much worse than they feel; it is there that all my sins and silliness are forgiven, there that I feel protected and at home, and every flower and weed is a friend and every tree a lover. When I have been vexed I run out to them for comfort, and when I have been angry without just cause, it is there that I find absolution. Did ever a woman have so many friends? And always the same, always ready to welcome me and fill me with cheerful thoughts. Happy children of a common Father, why should I, their own sister, be less content and joyous than they? Even in a thunder storm, when other people are running into the house, I run out of it. I do not like thunder storms﻿—they frighten me for hours before they come, because I always feel them on the way; but it is odd that I should go for shelter to the garden. I feel better there, more taken care of, more petted. When it thunders, the April baby says, “There’s *lieber Gott* scolding those angels again.” And once, when there was a storm in the night, she complained loudly, and wanted to know why *lieber Gott* didn’t do the scolding in the daytime, as she had been so *tight* asleep. They all three speak a wonderful mixture of German and English, adulterating the purity of their native tongue by putting in English words in the middle of a German sentence. It always reminds me of Justice tempered by Mercy.

===Chinese===
花园才是我去寻求庇护和遮蔽的地方，而不是房子。房子里有职责和烦扰，有需要规劝告诫的仆人，有家具，有一日三餐；而在外面，福气步步围拢着我——正是在那里，我为自己心里的刻薄难过，为那些自私的念头难过，它们远比感觉上坏得多；正是在那里，我一切的罪过和愚蠢都被赦免，在那里我感到被保护、如归家园，每一朵花、每一棵草都是朋友，每一棵树都是恋人。受了气，我跑到它们身边寻求安慰；无缘无故发了火，也是在它们那里求得宽赦。可有哪位女人拥有过这么多朋友？而且永远是同一批，永远随时欢迎我，让我心里满是愉快的念头。同一位天父的快乐儿女啊，我既是他们的亲姐妹，凭什么要比他们更不知足、更不欢喜呢？哪怕雷雨大作、别人纷纷往屋里跑的时候，我也是往外跑的。我并不喜欢雷雨——它们来之前好几小时就让我害怕，因为我总能觉出它们已在路上；可奇怪的是，我竟要到花园里去避难。在那儿我感觉更好，更受照顾，更被娇宠。打雷的时候，四月宝贝就说：「是*lieber Gott*（亲爱的上帝）又在训斥那些天使了。」有一回夜里打雷，她大声抱怨，想知道*lieber Gott*为什么不在白天训，因为她睡得那么*tight*（沉）。三个孩子都说着一种德语和英语的奇妙混合语，在德语句子中间嵌进英语词，败坏了母语的纯正。这总让我想起以慈悲调剂过的公义。

===Original===
We have been cowslipping today in a little wood dignified by the name of the Hirschwald, because it is the happy hunting-ground of innumerable deer who fight there in the autumn evenings, calling each other out to combat with bayings that ring through the silence and send agreeable shivers through the lonely listener. I often walk there in September, late in the evening, and sitting on a fallen tree listen fascinated to their angry cries.

We made cowslip balls sitting on the grass. The babies had never seen such things nor had imagined anything half so sweet. The Hirschwald is a little open wood of silver birches and springy turf starred with flowers, and there is a tiny stream meandering amiably about it and decking itself in June with yellow flags. I have dreams of having a little cottage built there, with the daisies up to the door, and no path of any sort﻿—just big enough to hold myself and one baby inside and a purple clematis outside. Two rooms﻿—a bedroom and a kitchen. How scared we would be at night, and how completely happy by day! I know the exact spot where it should stand, facing southeast, so that we should get all the cheerfulness of the morning, and close to the stream, so that we might wash our plates among the flags. Sometimes, when in the mood for society, we would invite the remaining babies to tea and entertain them with wild strawberries on plates of horse-chestnut leaves; but no one less innocent and easily pleased than a baby would be permitted to darken the effulgence of our sunny cottage﻿—indeed, I don’t suppose that anybody wiser would care to come. Wise people want so many things before they can even begin to enjoy themselves, and I feel perpetually apologetic when with them, for only being able to offer them that which I love best myself﻿—apologetic, and ashamed of being so easily contented.

===Chinese===
我们今天去采黄花九轮草了，在一片被尊称为希尔施瓦尔德（Hirschwald，德语「鹿林」）的小树林里；那里是无数鹿儿的安乐猎场，秋天的傍晚它们在那儿打架，以穿透寂静的吼声相互叫阵，让孤独的听者遍体生出快意的寒栗。九月里我常在深夜去那儿散步，坐在一株倒木上，出神地听它们愤怒的鸣叫。

我们坐在草地上打黄花九轮草花球。宝贝们从没见过这样的东西，也想象不出有比这甜上一半的玩意儿。希尔施瓦尔德是一片开阔的小树林，白桦亭亭，松软的草皮缀满星点小花，一条小溪和善地在林间蜿蜒，到了六月便给自己戴上黄菖蒲的装饰。我梦想在那儿盖一座小屋，雏菊一直长到门口，没有任何路径——大小刚好容下我和一个宝贝在里面，外面爬一株紫花铁线莲。两个房间——一间卧室，一间厨房。夜里我们该有多害怕，白天又该有多圆满的快乐啊！我知道它该盖的确切位置，朝向东南，好让我们独享清晨的全部明朗，又紧挨着小溪，好让我们在菖蒲之间洗盘子。有时兴致来了想交际，我们就把其余的宝贝们请来喝茶，用七叶树叶片当盘子、盛着野草莓招待她们；但任何不如幼儿天真、不如幼儿容易满足的人，都不许来遮暗我们阳光小屋的光辉——说实话，我也不认为更聪明的人会愿意来。聪明人要先把许许多多东西置办齐了才能开始享乐，跟他们在一起，我总觉得抱歉不停，因为我只能拿出我自己最爱的东西来招待他们——又抱歉，又为自己这么容易满足而羞愧。

===Original===
The other day at a dinner party in the nearest town (it took us the whole afternoon to get there) the women after dinner were curious to know how I had endured the winter, cut off from everybody and snowed up sometimes for weeks.

“Ah, these husbands!” sighed an ample lady, lugubriously shaking her head; “they shut up their wives because it suits them, and don’t care what their sufferings are.”

Then the others sighed and shook their heads too, for the ample lady was a great local potentate, and one began to tell how another dreadful husband had brought his young wife into the country and had kept her there, concealing her beauty and accomplishments from the public in a most cruel manner, and how, after spending a certain number of years in alternately weeping and producing progeny, she had quite lately run away with somebody unspeakable﻿—I think it was the footman, or the baker, or someone of that sort.

===Chinese===
前几天在最近的那个城里赴一场晚宴（光是到那儿就花去了我们整整一个下午），饭后太太们好奇地打听，与世隔绝、有时被大雪封困数星期的我，是怎么熬过这个冬天的。

「唉，这些做丈夫的！」一位丰腴的太太叹道，凄凄切切地摇着头；「他们把妻子关起来，因为这样对他们合适，才不管妻子受什么苦呢。」

于是旁的太太们也叹气、也摇头，因为这位丰腴的太太是本地一大权贵；接着有人开始讲述另一个可怕的丈夫，怎样把年轻的妻子带到乡下关在那里，以最残忍的方式把她的美貌和多才多艺对公众隐瞒起来；又讲她如何在哭了又哭、生了又生的若干年之后，前不久跟一个不可名状的人私奔了——我想那是男仆，要么就是面包师，诸如此类的什么人。

===Original===
“But I am quite happy,” I began, as soon as I could put in a word.

“Ah, a good little wife, making the best of it,” and the female potentate patted my hand, but continued gloomily to shake her head.

“You cannot possibly be happy in the winter entirely alone,” asserted another lady, the wife of a high military authority and not accustomed to be contradicted.

“But I am.”

“But how can you possibly be at your age? No, it is not possible.”

“But I *am*.”

===Chinese===
「可我很幸福啊，」我一找到插话的机会就说。

「啊，一位乖巧的小太太，随遇而安，」这位女藩王拍拍我的手，头却继续阴郁地摇着。

「你冬天孤零零一个人，不可能幸福，」另一位太太断言道，她是一位军方要人的妻子，不习惯被人反驳。

「可我确实幸福。」

「你这个年纪，怎么可能呢？不，不可能。」

「可我*就是*幸福。」

===Original===
“Your husband ought to bring you to town in the winter.”

“But I don’t want to be brought to town.”

“And not let you waste your best years buried.”

“But I like being buried.”

“Such solitude is not right.”

“But I’m not solitary.”

“And can come to no good.” She was getting quite angry.

There was a chorus of No Indeeds at her last remark, and renewed shaking of heads.

===Chinese===
「你丈夫冬天应该带你进城。」

「可我不想被带进城。」

「也不该让你把最好的年华埋在土里。」

「可我喜欢被埋着。」

「这样的孤独是不对的。」

「可我并不孤独。」

「而且不会有好结果。」她简直要生气了。

她最后这句话引来一片「可不是嘛」的齐声附和，还有重新摇起来的脑袋。

===Original===
“I enjoyed the winter immensely,” I persisted when they were a little quieter; “I sleighed and skated, and then there were the children, and shelves and shelves full of﻿—” I was going to say books, but stopped. Reading is an occupation for men; for women it is reprehensible waste of time. And how could I talk to them of the happiness I felt when the sun shone on the snow, or of the deep delight of hoarfrost days?

“It is entirely my doing that we have come down here,” I proceeded, “and my husband only did it to please me.”

“Such a good little wife,” repeated the patronising potentate, again patting my hand with an air of understanding all about it, “really an excellent little wife. But you must not let your husband have his own way too much, my dear, and take my advice and insist on his bringing you to town next winter.” And then they fell to talking about their cooks, having settled to their entire satisfaction that my fate was probably lying in wait for me too, lurking perhaps at that very moment behind the apparently harmless brass buttons of the man in the hall with my cloak.

===Chinese===
「我冬天过得快活极了，」等她们稍微安静些，我坚持道；「我坐雪橇、滑冰，还有孩子们，还有一架子一架子的——」我本想说「书」，但住了口。阅读是男人干的事；对女人来说，那是应受指摘的浪费时间。我又怎么能跟她们讲，阳光照在雪上时我感到的幸福，或白霜天气里那深深的喜悦呢？

「搬下来住完全是我的主意，」我接着说，「我丈夫这么做只是为了迁就我。」

「真是个乖巧的小太太，」这位居高临下的女藩王又说了一遍，再次拍拍我的手，一副全然了然的神气，「着实是位无可挑剔的小太太。不过你可不能太由着你丈夫，亲爱的，听我的劝，坚持要他明年冬天带你进城。」随后她们谈起各自的厨子——此时她们已经完全满意地断定，我的命运多半也在哪里埋伏着等我，说不定此刻正潜伏在大厅里那个替我拿斗篷的男人那看似无害的铜纽扣后面。

===Original===
I laughed on the way home, and I laughed again for sheer satisfaction when we reached the garden and drove between the quiet trees to the pretty old house; and when I went into the library, with its four windows open to the moonlight and the scent, and looked round at the familiar bookshelves, and could hear no sounds but sounds of peace, and knew that here I might read or dream or idle exactly as I chose with never a creature to disturb me, how grateful I felt to the kindly Fate that has brought me here and given me a heart to understand my own blessedness, and rescued me from a life like that I had just seen﻿—a life spent with the odours of other people’s dinners in one’s nostrils, and the noise of their wrangling servants in one’s ears, and parties and tattle for all amusement.

===Chinese===
回家的路上我一路笑，等我们驶进花园，在安静的树木之间赶向那座可爱的老宅时，我又纯粹出于满足笑了起来；而当我走进书房，四扇窗全部敞向月光与花香，环顾熟悉的书架，听见的唯有安宁的声息，知道在这里我可以随心所欲地读书、做梦、闲散，没有任何人来打扰，我对那把我带到这里、给了我一颗能领会自身福祉的心的仁慈命运，是多么感激啊——它把我从方才目睹的那种生活里救了出来——那种鼻孔里灌满别人家饭菜味、耳朵里灌满他们家仆人吵嚷声、以宴饮和闲话为全部消遣的生活。

===Original===
But I must confess to having felt sometimes quite crushed when some grand person, examining the details of my home through her eyeglass, and coolly dissecting all that I so much prize from the convenient distance of the open window, has finished up by expressing sympathy with my loneliness, and on my protesting that I like it, has murmured, “*sehr anspruchslos*.” Then indeed I have felt ashamed of the fewness of my wants; but only for a moment, and only under the withering influence of the eyeglass; for, after all, the owner’s spirit is the same spirit as that which dwells in my servants﻿—girls whose one idea of happiness is to live in a town where there are others of their sort with whom to drink beer and dance on Sunday afternoons. The passion for being forever with one’s fellows, and the fear of being left for a few hours alone, is to me wholly incomprehensible. I can entertain myself quite well for weeks together, hardly aware, except for the pervading peace, that I have been alone at all. Not but what I like to have people staying with me for a few days, or even for a few weeks, should they be as *anspruchslos* as I am myself, and content with simple joys; only, anyone who comes here and would be happy must have something in him; if he be a mere blank creature, empty of head and heart, he will very probably find it dull. I should like my house to be often full if I could find people capable of enjoying themselves. They should be welcomed and sped with equal heartiness; for truth compels me to confess that, though it pleases me to see them come, it pleases me just as much to see them go.

===Chinese===
不过我得承认，有时某位显赫人物隔着单片眼镜审视我家的一切细节，站在敞开的窗口那便利的距离上，冷冷地解剖我所珍爱的一切，末了还对我的孤寂表示同情，而在我抗议说我就喜欢这样时，低声吐出一句「*sehr anspruchslos*（真是不讲究啊）」——每逢这种时候，我确实感到相当被碾压。那一刻我当真为自己欲求之少而羞愧；但也只是一时，且只在单片眼镜那摧人威压之下；因为说到底，这位主人的精神，与我仆人体内栖居的乃是同一种精神——姑娘们对幸福的全部想象，就是住在一个城里，那里有她们的同类，可以一起喝啤酒，在星期天下午跳舞。永远与同伴厮守的热望，和怕被独自撇下几个小时的恐慌，在我看来全然不可理解。我可以接连几个星期自娱自乐，若不是那弥漫四方的安宁，几乎意识不到自己一直是独自一人。这并不是说我不喜欢有人来小住几日，甚至小住几周——只要他们同我一样*anspruchslos*（不事讲究），满足于简单的快乐就行；只是，凡是来这里还想过得快活的人，肚子里得有点东西；倘若他只是个空壳生物，头脑和心肠都空空如也，他很可能会觉得无聊。倘若我能找到会自得其乐的人，我倒愿意让房子常常客满。他们来时我当热情欢迎，去时也同样热情相送；因为真相逼我承认：虽然看见他们来令我欢喜，看见他们走也令我同样欢喜。

===Original===
On some very specially divine days, like today, I have actually longed for someone else to be here to enjoy the beauty with me. There has been rain in the night, and the whole garden seems to be singing﻿—not the untiring birds only, but the vigorous plants, the happy grass and trees, the lilac bushes﻿—oh, those lilac bushes! They are all out today, and the garden is drenched with the scent. I have brought in armfuls, the picking is such a delight, and every pot and bowl and tub in the house is filled with purple glory, and the servants think there is going to be a party and are extra nimble, and I go from room to room gazing at the sweetness, and the windows are all flung open so as to join the scent within to the scent without; and the servants gradually discover that there is no party, and wonder why the house should be filled with flowers for one woman by herself, and I long more and more for a kindred spirit﻿—it seems so greedy to have so much loveliness to oneself﻿—but kindred spirits are so very, very rare; I might almost as well cry for the moon. It is true that my garden is full of friends, only they are﻿—dumb.

===Chinese===
在有些格外神圣的日子里，比如今天，我当真渴望有另一个人在这里，同我一起享受这份美。夜里下过雨，整个花园仿佛都在歌唱——不止是不知疲倦的鸟儿，还有蓬勃的草木、欢欣的花草树丛、丁香丛——哦，那些丁香丛！它们今天全都开了，花园浸透了那芬芳。我抱进来一捧又一捧，采摘本身就是这样一种快乐，家里每一只瓶、碗、盆都盛满了紫色的荣光，仆人们以为要开宴会，手脚格外麻利，我在一个个房间之间穿行，凝望这满室的甜香，窗子全都敞开，好让屋里的芬芳与屋外的芬芳连成一片；仆人们渐渐发现并没有什么宴会，纳闷这房子为什么要为一个独自居住的女人插满鲜花，而我越来越渴望一个知音——独占这么多可爱，未免太贪心——可知音是那样、那样稀少；我几乎还不如对着月亮哭喊。的确，我的花园满是朋友，只可惜它们——不会说话。

## June 3rd / 6月3日

===Original===
This is such an out-of-the-way corner of the world that it requires quite unusual energy to get here at all, and I am thus delivered from casual callers; while, on the other hand, people I love, or people who love me, which is much the same thing, are not likely to be deterred from coming by the roundabout train journey and the long drive at the end. Not the least of my many blessings is that we have only one neighbour. If you have to have neighbours at all, it is at least a mercy that there should be only one; for with people dropping in at all hours and wanting to talk to you, how are you to get on with your life, I should like to know, and read your books, and dream your dreams to your satisfaction? Besides, there is always the certainty that either you or the dropper-in will say something that would have been better left unsaid, and I have a holy horror of gossip and mischief-making. A woman’s tongue is a deadly weapon and the most difficult thing in the world to keep in order, and things slip off it with a facility nothing short of appalling at the very moment when it ought to be most quiet. In such cases the only safe course is to talk steadily about cooks and children, and to pray that the visit may not be too prolonged, for if it is you are lost. Cooks I have found to be the best of all subjects﻿—the most phlegmatic flush into life at the mere word, and the joys and sufferings connected with them are experiences common to us all.

===Chinese===
这里是世界上一个如此偏僻的角落，要想抵达此地，非得有格外不寻常的精力不可，我因此得免于不速之客的叨扰；而另一方面，我爱的人，或爱我的人——这两者本是一回事——也不至于被绕来绕去的火车旅程和终点前那段长长的车程吓退，不肯前来。在我诸多的福气当中，不小的一项便是我们只有一位邻居。既然邻居终究免不了，那么只有一位至少算是桩幸事；因为要是有人不分时辰地登门串扰、非要同你说话不可，请问你还怎么过自己的日子、读自己的书、称心如意地做自己的梦？何况总有一桩铁定的事：不是你就是那位不速之客，准会说出些不如不说的话来，而我对说长道短、搬弄是非，怀着一种神圣的恐惧。女人的舌头是一件致命的武器，是世上最难管束的东西，偏偏在最该缄默的时刻，它会以简直骇人的灵巧溜出一串又一串话来。在这种情形下，唯一稳妥的对策便是絮絮不休地大谈厨娘和孩子，然后祈祷这一趟来访别拖得太久，因为一旦拖久了，你就完了。我发现厨娘是所有话题里最好的一个——最迟钝的人一听这两个字也会满面生辉，而与厨娘相连的甘苦，更是我们人人共有的阅历。

===Original===
Luckily, our neighbour and his wife are both busy and charming, with a whole troop of flaxen-haired little children to keep them occupied, besides the business of their large estate. Our intercourse is arranged on lines of the most beautiful simplicity. I call on her once a year, and she returns the call a fortnight later; they ask us to dinner in the summer, and we ask them to dinner in the winter. By strictly keeping to this, we avoid all danger of that closer friendship which is only another name for frequent quarrels. She is a pattern of what a German country lady should be, and is not only a pretty woman but an energetic and practical one, and the combination is, to say the least, effective. She is up at daylight superintending the feeding of the stock, the butter-making, the sending off of the milk for sale; a thousand things get done while most people are fast asleep, and before lazy folk are well at breakfast she is off in her pony-carriage to the other farms on the place, to rate the “mamsells,” as the head women are called, to poke into every corner, lift the lids off the saucepans, count the new-laid eggs, and box, if necessary, any careless dairymaid’s ears. We are allowed by law to administer “slight corporal punishment” to our servants, it being left entirely to individual taste to decide what “slight” shall be, and my neighbour really seems to enjoy using this privilege, judging from the way she talks about it. I would give much to be able to peep through a keyhole and see the dauntless little lady, terrible in her wrath and dignity, standing on tiptoe to box the ears of some great strapping girl big enough to eat her.

===Chinese===
所幸我们的邻居夫妇俩既忙碌又可爱，除了一大份田产要操持，还有一整队亚麻色头发的小孩儿缠着他们。我们的交往安排在最美妙简洁的轨道上：我每年拜访她一次，她两周后回拜；夏天他们请我们吃饭，冬天我们请他们吃饭。严格恪守这一套，我们便避开了一切危险，不会发展出那种更亲密的友谊——而亲密友谊不过是频繁争吵的别名罢了。她是德国乡绅太太的典范，不但人长得俊俏，而且精力充沛、讲究实干，这两样凑在一起，至少可以说，成效惊人。天一亮她便起身，督着喂牲口、做黄油、把牛奶发出去卖；在大多数人酣睡未醒的时候，一千件事已经办妥；等懒人们刚坐上早饭，她已坐着小马车到庄园里另外几座农庄去了，训斥那些被唤作「*mamsells*」的女管家，每个角落都要探一探，每只锅盖都要掀一掀，新下的鸡蛋要数一数，遇上哪个挤奶女工马虎大意，必要时还要刮她的耳朵。法律允许我们对仆人施以「轻微体罚」，至于何谓「轻微」，全凭各人品味自定；从她谈起这事时的那副口气听来，我的邻居实在像是颇乐于行使这项特权。我肯出大价钱换一个机会，从钥匙孔里偷看一眼那位无所畏惧的小个子太太——她怒气冲冲、威风凛凛，踮起脚尖去刮某个高大健壮的姑娘的耳朵，那姑娘块头大得几乎能把她一口吞下。

===Original===
The making of cheese and butter and sausages *excellently* well is a work which requires brains, and is, to my thinking, a very admirable form of activity, and entirely worthy of the attention of the intelligent. That my neighbour is intelligent is at once made evident by the bright alertness of her eyes﻿—eyes that nothing escapes, and that only gain in prettiness by being used to some good purpose. She is a recognised authority for miles around on the mysteries of sausage-making, the care of calves, and the slaughtering of swine; and with all her manifold duties and daily prolonged absences from home, her children are patterns of health and neatness, and of what dear little German children, with white pigtails and fearless eyes and thick legs, should be. Who shall say that such a life is sordid and dull and unworthy of a high order of intelligence? I protest that to me it is a beautiful life, full of wholesome outdoor work, and with no room for those listless moments of depression and boredom, and of wondering what you will do next, that leave wrinkles round a pretty woman’s eyes, and are not unknown even to the most brilliant. But while admiring my neighbour, I don’t think I shall ever try to follow in her steps, my talents not being of the energetic and organising variety, but rather of that order which makes their owner almost lamentably prone to take up a volume of poetry and wander out to where the kingcups grow, and, sitting on a willow trunk beside a little stream, forget the very existence of everything but green pastures and still waters, and the glad blowing of the wind across the joyous fields. And it would make me perfectly wretched to be confronted by ears so refractory as to require boxing.

===Chinese===
把干酪、黄油和香肠做得*格外*地道，是一桩需要头脑的活计，在我看来，也是一种极可敬的活动方式，完全当得起聪明人的关注。我的邻居是否聪明，从她那双眼睛的机敏明亮里立刻可以看出——那是一双什么也逃不过的眼睛，而且因为派上了正经用场，反倒愈发添了姿色。方圆数英里之内，她是公认的权威，精通香肠制作的奥秘、牛犊的照料和生猪的宰杀；尽管她事务繁冗、每天长时间离家在外，她的孩子们却是个个健康整洁，是可爱的德国小孩子——白白的辫子、无畏的眼睛、结实的腿——应有的模范。谁敢说这样的生活卑琐、乏味、配不上高等的智力？我敢说，在我看来这是一种美丽的生活，充满有益健康的户外劳作，容不下那些无精打采的消沉与无聊的时刻——那种不知接下来该做什么的茫然，会在漂亮女人的眼角刻下皱纹，而且纵使最出色的人物也未能全然幸免。不过，尽管钦佩我的邻居，我想我永远不会去步她的后尘，因为我的才能不属于精力充沛、善于统筹的那一类，而属于另外一种——这才能使它的主人几乎可悲地动不动就挟起一卷诗，信步走到驴蹄草（kingcup）盛开的地方，在溪畔一段柳树干上坐下，忘掉天地间万事万物，只剩下青青草场、静静流水，和掠过欢乐田野的浩荡长风。再说，一想到要面对那样一副顽劣得非刮不可的耳朵，我就会沮丧透顶。

===Original===
Sometimes callers from a distance invade my solitude, and it is on these occasions that I realise how absolutely alone each individual is, and how far away from his neighbour; and while they talk (generally about babies, past, present, and to come), I fall to wondering at the vast and impassable distance that separates one’s own soul from the soul of the person sitting in the next chair. I am speaking of comparative strangers, people who are forced to stay a certain time by the eccentricities of trains, and in whose presence you grope about after common interests and shrink back into your shell on finding that you have none. Then a frost slowly settles down on me and I grow each minute more benumbed and speechless, and the babies feel the frost in the air and look vacant, and the callers go through the usual form of wondering who they most take after, generally settling the question by saying that the May baby, who is the beauty, is like her father, and that the two more or less plain ones are the image of me, and this decision, though I know it of old and am sure it is coming, never fails to depress me as much as though I heard it for the first time. The babies are very little and inoffensive and good, and it is hard that they should be used as a means of filling up gaps in conversation, and their features pulled to pieces one by one, and all their weak points noted and criticised, while they stand smiling shyly in the operator’s face, their very smile drawing forth comments on the shape of their mouths; but, after all, it does not occur very often, and they are one of those few interests one has in common with other people, as everybody seems to have babies. A garden, I have discovered, is by no means a fruitful topic, and it is amazing how few persons really love theirs﻿—they all pretend they do, but you can hear by the very tone of their voice what a lukewarm affection it is. About June their interest is at its warmest, nourished by agreeable supplies of strawberries and roses; but on reflection I don’t know a single person within twenty miles who really cares for his garden, or has discovered the treasures of happiness that are buried in it, and are to be found if sought for diligently, and if needs be with tears. It is after these rare calls that I experience the only moments of depression from which I ever suffer, and then I am angry at myself, a well-nourished person, for allowing even a single precious hour of life to be spoilt by anything so indifferent. That is the worst of being fed enough, and clothed enough, and warmed enough, and of having everything you can reasonably desire﻿—on the least provocation you are made uncomfortable and unhappy by such abstract discomforts as being shut out from a nearer approach to your neighbour’s soul; which is on the face of it foolish, the probability being that he hasn’t got one.

===Chinese===
有时，远方来的访客会侵入我的清静，正是在这种时刻，我才体会到每个人是何等彻底的孤独，与他的邻人相隔何等遥远；当他们高谈阔论的时候（谈的通常是婴儿，过去的、现在的、将来的），我便开始惊叹那横亘在自己灵魂与邻座之人灵魂之间的、辽阔而不可逾越的距离。我这里说的是那些泛泛之交——被火车的古怪时刻逼得不得不多坐一阵的人，在他们面前，你摸索着搜寻共同的话题，一旦发现无从搜寻，便缩回自己的壳里。于是一层寒霜慢慢落到我身上，我一分比一分更麻木、更无言，宝贝们也觉出空气里的寒意，神情发怔；访客们照例把那套功课走一遍，猜她们最像谁，而结论多半是：最美的五月宝贝像她父亲，另外两个多少平庸的活脱脱是我的翻版——这判决我虽早已烂熟、明知它必来，却每次都像头一回听见似的叫我沮丧。宝贝们年纪幼小，与世无争，乖乖巧巧，却要被拿来填补谈话的空白，五官被一件一件拆开来评点，所有的短处都被留意、被批评，而她们只能羞怯地冲着那位品评者微笑，连那一笑本身也要招来对她们嘴形的品头论足，这实在叫人难受；不过好在这种事不常发生，而且她们毕竟属于人们少有的共同兴趣之一，因为看上去人人都有孩子。一座花园，我发现，绝不是个丰盛的话题，真正爱自家园子的人少得惊人——人人都装作爱，可你光听那声调便知道那是一种怎样温吞的感情。到了六月，他们的兴头最热，靠的是草莓和月季源源不断、可人心意的供应；可细想一下，方圆二十英里之内，我说不出有哪一个人真正在乎他的园子，或者发现过埋藏在园中的幸福宝藏——那些宝藏，只要勤勤恳恳去寻，必要时含着泪去寻，总能寻见。正是在这些稀罕的来访之后，我才会经历平生仅有的消沉时刻；随后我便生自己的气——一个吃得饱的人，竟允许哪怕一个钟头的宝贵光阴被这样无谓的事情败坏。这就是吃得饱、穿得暖、住得暖、凡属合理的愿望一概不缺的坏处——稍受撩拨，你便会为种种虚无缥缈的不快而不自在、不幸福，比如无法再靠近邻人的灵魂一步；这明摆着是愚蠢的，因为十有八九，他压根儿就没有灵魂。

===Original===
The rockets are all out. The gardener, in a fit of inspiration, put them right along the very front of two borders, and I don’t know what his feelings can be now that they are all flowering and the plants behind are completely hidden; but I have learned another lesson, and no future gardener shall be allowed to run riot among my rockets in quite so reckless a fashion. They are charming things, as delicate in colour as in scent, and a bowl of them on my writing-table fills the room with fragrance. Single rows, however, are a mistake; I had masses of them planted in the grass, and these show how lovely they can be. A border full of rockets, mauve and white, and nothing else, must be beautiful; but I don’t know how long they last nor what they look like when they have done flowering. This I shall find out in a week or two, I suppose. Was ever a would-be gardener left so entirely to his own blundering? No doubt it would be a gain of years to the garden if I were not forced to learn solely by my failures, and if I had some kind creature to tell me when to do things. At present the only flowers in the garden are the rockets, the pansies in the rose beds, and two groups of azaleas﻿—mollis and pontica. The azaleas have been and still are gorgeous; I only planted them this spring and they almost at once began to flower, and the sheltered corner they are in looks as though it were filled with imprisoned and perpetual sunsets. Orange, lemon, pink in every delicate shade﻿—what they will be next year and in succeeding years when the bushes are bigger, I can imagine from the way they have begun life. On gray, dull days the effect is absolutely startling. Next autumn I shall make a great bank of them in front of a belt of fir trees in rather a gloomy nook. My tea-roses are covered with buds which will not open for at least another week, so I conclude this is not the sort of climate where they will flower from the very beginning of June to November, as they are said to do.

===Chinese===
香紫罗兰（rocket）全都开了。园丁一时灵感迸发，把它们栽在两条花境的最前排，如今它们全都开了花，把后面的植株遮了个严严实实，不知他此刻作何感想；不过我又上了一课，往后的园丁再也休想如此肆无忌惮地在我的一片香紫罗兰里撒野。它们是迷人的东西，色泽之淡雅一如芬芳之清幽，书桌上一碗香紫罗兰，便满室生香。只是单行栽种是个错误；我叫人在草地上成片成片地种了一些，这些才显出它们可以有多可爱。整整一畦香紫罗兰，紫的白的，旁的一概不杂，必定很美；只是我不知道它们能开多久，也不知花谢之后是什么光景。大概再过一两个星期便见分晓了。哪一个立志园艺的人，曾被如此彻底地丢给自己的瞎摸乱撞呢？倘若我不至于全靠失败来学习，倘若有哪个好心的生灵肯指点我什么时候该做什么，园子无疑能少走好几年弯路。眼下园中开花的只有香紫罗兰、月季床里的三色堇，还有两丛杜鹃——*mollis* 和 *pontica*。那两丛杜鹃此前至今一直绚烂极了；我今年春天才把它们种下，它们几乎立刻就开了花，它们所在的那个避风角落，看上去像是盛满了一场场被囚禁的、永不落幕的落日。橙色、柠檬色、深深浅浅的粉——来年以及往后灌木长成之后会是何等景象，看看它们今日开局的气派便可想见。在灰蒙蒙的阴天里，那效果真是惊人。明年秋天，我要在一带冷杉前、一个相当幽暗的角落里，为它们堆起一道大大的花坡。我的茶香月季缀满了花苞，至少还要再等一个星期才开，于是我断定，这里并不是那种据说能让它们从六月初一直开到十一月去的气候。

## July 11th / 7月11日

===Original===
There has been no rain since the day before Whitsunday, five weeks ago, which partly, but not entirely, accounts for the disappointment my beds have been. The dejected gardener went mad soon after Whitsuntide, and had to be sent to an asylum. He took to going about with a spade in one hand and a revolver in the other, explaining that he felt safer that way, and we bore it quite patiently, as becomes civilised beings who respect each other’s prejudices, until one day, when I mildly asked him to tie up a fallen creeper﻿—and after he bought the revolver my tones in addressing him were of the mildest, and I quite left off reading to him aloud﻿—he turned round, looked me straight in the face for the first time since he has been here, and said, “Do I look like Graf X﻿—﻿— (a great local celebrity), or like a monkey?” After which there was nothing for it but to get him into an asylum as expeditiously as possible. There was no gardener to be had in his place, and I have only just succeeded in getting one; so that what with the drought, and the neglect, and the gardener’s madness, and my blunders, the garden is in a sad condition; but even in a sad condition it is the dearest place in the world, and all my mistakes only make me more determined to persevere.

===Chinese===
自圣灵降临日前一天起就滴雨未下，已经五个星期了，这多少能解释——但并不能完全解释——我的花床何以如此令人失望。那位郁郁寡欢的园丁在圣灵降临节后不久疯了，只好被送进疯人院。他养成了一手拿铲子、一手拿左轮枪四处走动的习惯，还解释说这样觉得安全些；我们相当耐心地容忍了这一点，正如尊重彼此偏见的文明人所当为——直到有一天，我温和地请他把一株倒伏的藤蔓扶起来绑好——自从他买了左轮枪，我对他说话的调子都是最温和的，也彻底不再念书给他听了——他转过身来，自来此之后头一回直直地盯着我的脸看，说：「我看起来像某伯爵（Graf X——，本地一位大名鼎鼎的人物）呢，还是像一只猴子？」此后就别无他法，只能尽快把他弄进疯人院去。他走后一时找不到园丁接替，我到最近才算找到一个；于是乎，旱灾、失管、园丁发疯，加上我的种种失误，花园落到凄惨的境地；可即便是凄惨的境地，它也是世上最亲爱的地方，而我犯的每一个错，只会让我更下定决心坚持下去。

===Original===
The long borders, where the rockets were, are looking dreadful. The rockets have done flowering, and, after the manner of rockets in other walks of life, have degenerated into sticks; and nothing else in those borders intends to bloom this summer. The giant poppies I had planted out in them in April have either died off or remained quite small, and so have the columbines; here and there a delphinium droops unwillingly, and that is all. I suppose poppies cannot stand being moved, or perhaps they were not watered enough at the time of transplanting; anyhow, those borders are going to be sown tomorrow with more poppies for next year; for poppies I will have, whether they like it or not, and they shall not be touched, only thinned out.

Well, it is no use being grieved, and after all, directly I come out and sit under the trees, and look at the dappled sky, and see the sunshine on the cornfields away on the plain, all the disappointment smooths itself out, and it seems impossible to be sad and discontented when everything about me is so radiant and kind.

===Chinese===
原先种着香紫罗兰的那几条长花境，如今惨不忍睹。香紫罗兰花事已了，而且像别的行当里的「火箭」一样，退化成了一根根光杆儿；那些花境里的其他东西，今年夏天一概不打算开花。我四月里移栽进去的大罂粟，要么死掉了，要么一直长得很小，耧斗菜也一样；东一株西一株的翠雀勉强垂着头，仅此而已。我想罂粟是经不起移栽的，要么就是移栽时水没浇够；不管怎样，明天那几条花境要再播下更多罂粟，留给明年；因为罂粟我是非要不可的，不管它们乐意不乐意，而且到时候谁也不许碰它们，只许间苗。

唉，伤心也没有用；毕竟，我一走出来坐到树下，望着斑驳的天空，看见阳光洒在平原远处的麦田上，所有的失望便自己抚平了。四周的一切都这样明晃晃、这样和善，人似乎不可能再悲伤、再不满。

===Original===
Today is Sunday, and the garden is so quiet, that, sitting here in this shady corner watching the lazy shadows stretching themselves across the grass, and listening to the rooks quarrelling in the treetops, I almost expect to hear English church bells ringing for the afternoon service. But the church is three miles off, has no bells, and no afternoon service. Once a fortnight we go to morning prayer at eleven and sit up in a sort of private box with a room behind, whither we can retire unobserved when the sermon is too long or our flesh too weak, and hear ourselves being prayed for by the black-robed parson. In winter the church is bitterly cold; it is not heated, and we sit muffled up in more furs than ever we wear out of doors; but it would of course be very wicked for the parson to wear furs, however cold he may be, so he puts on a great many extra coats under his gown, and, as the winter progresses, swells to a prodigious size. We know when spring is coming by the reduction in his figure. The congregation sit at ease while the parson does the praying for them, and while they are droning the long-drawn-out chorales, he retires into a little wooden box just big enough to hold him. He does not come out until he thinks we have sung enough, nor do we stop until his appearance gives us the signal. I have often thought how dreadful it would be if he fell ill in his box and left us to go on singing. I am sure we should never dare to stop, unauthorised by the Church. I asked him once what he did in there; he looked very shocked at such a profane question, and made an evasive reply.

===Chinese===
今天是星期天，花园安静得很，坐在这荫凉的角落里，看着懒洋洋的影子在草地上缓缓伸展，听着白嘴鸦在树顶上争吵，我几乎要以为会听见英国教堂为午后礼拜鸣响的钟声了。可教堂在三英里外，没有钟，也没有午后礼拜。我们每两周去一次十一点的晨祷，坐在一种带后间的专属包厢里，布道太长或肉体太弱的时候，可以悄悄退进后间无人察觉，听那黑袍牧师为我们祈祷。冬天教堂冷得刺骨；没有暖气，我们裹的皮草比在户外穿的还多；但牧师穿皮草当然是非常有罪的，不管他多冷，所以他只好在长袍底下加了一件又一件衣服，随着冬天渐深，膨胀到惊人的块头。我们知道春天要来了，是看他的身形缩小了。会众们安安逸逸地坐着，祈祷的事由牧师代劳；当他们哼哼唧唧唱着拖得老长的众赞歌时，他便退进一只刚够容身的小木箱里。他不认为我们唱够了就不出来，我们不看到他露面也不敢停。我常想，倘若他在箱子里病倒了，留着我们一直唱下去，那该有多可怕。我敢肯定，没有教会的授权，我们是绝不敢停下来的。有一次我问他，他在里面做什么；他对这个大不敬的问题露出极为震惊的神色，给了一个搪塞的回答。

===Original===
If it were not for the garden, a German Sunday would be a terrible day; but in the garden on that day there is a sigh of relief and more profound peace, nobody raking or sweeping or fidgeting; only the little flowers themselves and the whispering trees.

===Chinese===
若不是有这花园，德国的星期天会是可怕的一天；但在星期天的花园里，有一声如释重负的叹息和更深沉的安宁，没有人耙叶，没有人扫地，没有人躁动；只有小小的花儿们和窃窃私语的树。

===Original===
I have been much afflicted again lately by visitors﻿—not stray callers to be got rid of after a due administration of tea and things you are sorry afterwards that you said, but people staying in the house and not to be got rid of at all. All June was lost to me in this way, and it was from first to last a radiant month of heat and beauty; but a garden where you meet the people you saw at breakfast, and will see again at lunch and dinner, is not a place to be happy in. Besides, they had a knack of finding out my favourite seats and lounging in them just when I longed to lounge myself; and they took books out of the library with them, and left them face downwards on the seats all night to get well drenched with dew, though they might have known that what is meat for roses is poison for books; and they gave me to understand that if they had had the arranging of the garden it would have been finished long ago﻿—whereas I don’t believe a garden ever is finished. They have all gone now, thank heaven, except one, so that I have a little breathing space before others begin to arrive. It seems that the place interests people, and that there is a sort of novelty in staying in such a deserted corner of the world, for they were in a perpetual state of mild amusement at being here at all.

===Chinese===
近来我又被访客们大大地折磨了一番——不是那种敬完茶、说过几句事后会后悔的话就能打发走的零散来客，而是住在家里的、根本打发不走的人。整个六月就这样从我手里丢掉了，而那从头到尾都是酷热而美丽、光辉灿烂的一个月；可是在一个早餐时见过、午餐晚餐还要再见的花园里，人是快活不起来的。何况，他们有种本事，专能找到我最心爱的座位，恰在我自己想瘫上去的时候瘫在上面；他们把书房里的书带出去，脸朝下摊在座位上过夜，让露水好好浸个透湿，尽管他们本该知道，于玫瑰是美餐的，于书是毒药；他们还让我明白，倘若花园交由他们来布置，早就弄完了——可我根本不相信有哪座花园是能弄完的。感谢上天，如今他们都走了，只剩一位，让我在下一批人到来之前有了一点喘息的空当。看来这地方让人们觉得有趣，住在世上这样一个被遗弃的角落里自有一番新鲜劲儿，因为单是「来到了这里」这件事，就让他们处于一种持续的、淡淡的乐趣之中。

===Original===
Irais is the only one left. She is a young woman with a beautiful, refined face, and her eyes and straight, fine eyebrows are particularly lovable. At meals she dips her bread into the saltcellar, bites a bit off, and repeats the process, although providence (taking my shape) has caused salt-spoons to be placed at convenient intervals down the table. She lunched today on beer, *Schweinekoteletten*, and cabbage-salad with caraway seeds in it, and now I hear her through the open window, extemporising touching melodies in her charming, cooing voice. She is thin, frail, intelligent, and lovable, all on the above diet. What better proof can be needed to establish the superiority of the Teuton than the fact that after such meals he can produce such music? Cabbage salad is a horrid invention, but I don’t doubt its utility as a means of encouraging thoughtfulness; nor will I quarrel with it, since it results so poetically, any more than I quarrel with the manure that results in roses, and I give it to Irais every day to make her sing. She is the sweetest singer I have ever heard, and has a charming trick of making up songs as she goes along. When she begins, I go and lean out of the window and look at my little friends out there in the borders while listening to her music, and feel full of pleasant sadness and regret. It is so sweet to be sad when one has nothing to be sad about.

===Chinese===
留下来的只有伊蕾斯。她是位有着美丽、清秀面庞的少妇，那双眼睛和又直又细的眉毛尤其可爱。吃饭时，她把面包伸进盐碟里蘸一蘸，咬掉一小口，然后再来一遍，尽管天意（取了我的形）已在桌上便利的间隔处摆好了小盐匙。她今天的午餐是啤酒、*Schweinekoteletten*（猪排），和加了葛缕子的卷心菜沙拉，此刻我隔着敞开的窗听见她正用那迷人的、鸽子般咕咕的嗓音即兴编唱着动人的曲调。她瘦削、纤弱、聪慧、可爱，全靠上述饮食养出来。要证明条顿人的优越，还需要什么比这更好的证据呢——吃完那样的饭，竟能产出这样的音乐？卷心菜沙拉是项可憎的发明，但我不怀疑它作为培养沉思之器的功效；也不会跟它过不去，既然它能结出如此诗意的结果，正如我不会跟养出玫瑰的粪肥过不去，何况我天天把它端给伊蕾斯，好让她唱歌。她是我听过的最甜美的歌手，还有一种边唱边现编歌儿的迷人本领。她一开腔，我就走到窗边探身出去，一边听她的歌，一边望着花境里我的小朋友们，满心愉快的哀愁和怅惘。当人本没有什么可愁的时候，发愁竟是这样甜。

===Original===
The April baby came panting up just as I had written that, the others hurrying along behind, and with flaming cheeks displayed for my admiration three brand-new kittens, lean and blind, that she was carrying in her pinafore, and that had just been found motherless in the woodshed.

“Look,” she cried breathlessly, “such a much!”
