With great presence of mind I instantly put my muff in front of it and held it there tight, while I listened. Dead silence, except for the laboured breathing and struggles of the little girl.

“I don’t hear a sound,” I whispered, letting her go again. “Now what did you want to say?” I added, eyeing her severely.

“I wanted to say,” she panted, “that it’s no good pretending you wash with a nose like that.”

===Chinese===
「而且他们从来不洗澡，罗宾逊小姐说的。你也不洗的，对不对？」

「不洗澡？哎呀，恐怕你真是个教养很坏的小姑娘——哦，别缠着我——我得跑了——」

「我也得跑，」小姑娘兴高采烈地说，「因为罗宾逊小姐准就在我们后头。我找到你之前，差一点被她逮着。」说着她便在我旁边跑了起来。

一想到罗宾逊小姐紧追在后，我的脚上便生出了翅膀；我把仅剩无几的体面也抛给了风，顺着步道飞奔起来。小姑娘偏偏又跑得过人，尽管气喘吁吁、脸色变出种种古怪的颜色，仍旧跟在我旁边，居然还有说话的余力。唉，我累了，身心俱疲，被接连的惊吓累坏了，被旅程累坏了，被没吃上饭累坏了；如今竟还被迫跑步，只因为这么个顽劣透顶的小姑娘不肯进去上课，偏要躲起来。

「我说——真痛快——」她一句一顿地蹦出话来。

「可我们干吗要跑到同一个地方去呢？」我气喘吁吁地问，指望能甩掉她，纯属妄想。

「哦，正是——这才叫——有意思。咱们俩——正好搭伴——你和我——」

「不成，不成，」我说，这一点上主意拿得铁定，尽管人还晕头转向。

「我也受不了——洗澡——冬天洗澡——可怕死了——还会让人——生皴。」

「可我一点也不在乎洗澡，」我有气无力地抗议，身上再没剩半点力气。

「哎呀，我说！」小姑娘看着我的脸，发出一种俗称大笑的声音。这小姑娘的没上没下，实在叫人反感透了。

我们平安穿过了那道门，绕过小萝卜占着的那个拐角，进了灌木林。凭经验我知道，在这小径交错的地方藏起来是多么容易，便停下脚步，四下张望、侧耳细听。小姑娘张嘴要说话。我当机立断，立刻把皮手筒捂在她嘴上，紧紧按住，一面听。四下死寂，只有小姑娘费劲的呼吸和挣扎。

「我什么也没听见，」我松开她，低声说，「好了，你刚才想说什么？」我接着问，狠狠地瞪着她。

「我想说的是，」她喘着气说，「长着那样一个鼻子，还硬装你洗澡，是没有用的。」

===Original===
“A nose like that! A nose like what?” I exclaimed, greatly offended; and though I put up my hand and very tenderly and carefully felt it, I could find no difference in it. “I am afraid poor Miss Robinson must have a wretched life,” I said, in tones of deep disgust.

The little girl smiled fatuously, as though I were paying her compliments. “It’s all green and brown,” she said, pointing. “Is it always like that?”

Then I remembered the wet fir tree near the gate, and the enraptured kiss it had received, and blushed.

“Won’t it come off?” persisted the little girl.

“Of course it will come off,” I answered, frowning.

“Why don’t you rub it off?”

Then I remembered the throwing away of the handkerchief, and blushed again.

“Please lend me your handkerchief,” I said humbly, “I﻿—I have lost mine.”

There was a great fumbling in six different pockets, and then a handkerchief that made me young again merely to look at it was produced. I took it thankfully and rubbed with energy, the little girl, intensely interested, watching the operation and giving me advice. “There﻿—it’s all right now﻿—a little more on the right﻿—there﻿—now it’s all off.”

“Are you sure? No green left?” I anxiously asked.

“No, it’s red all over now,” she replied cheerfully. “Let me get home,” thought I, very much upset by this information, “let me get home to my dear, uncritical, admiring babies, who accept my nose as an example of what a nose should be, and whatever its colour think it beautiful.” And thrusting the handkerchief back into the little girl’s hands, I hurried away down the path. She packed it away hastily, but it took some seconds for it was of the size of a small sheet, and then came running after me. “Where are you going?” she asked surprised, as I turned down the path leading to the gate.

“Through this gate,” I replied with decision.

“But you mustn’t﻿—we’re not allowed to go through there﻿—”

===Chinese===
「那样一个鼻子！哪样的鼻子？」我大声说，大为光火；尽管我抬手极其温柔、极其小心地摸了摸它，也没摸出它有什么两样。「恐怕可怜的罗宾逊小姐日子一定过得很惨，」我说，语气里满是深深的嫌恶。

小姑娘傻乎乎地笑了，仿佛我在夸她似的。「它整个儿是绿的加棕的，」她指了指说。「它一直是这样的吗？」

这时我想起门边那棵湿冷杉，和它受到的那一记如痴如醉的吻，脸红了。

「它擦不掉吗？」小姑娘追问不休。

「当然擦得掉，」我皱着眉答道。

「那你干吗不擦掉？」

于是我又想起那块被扔掉的手帕，脸再次红了。

「请把你的手帕借我用用，」我低声下气地说，「我——我把我的弄丢了。」

她浑身上下六只口袋一阵好摸，最后掏出一块手帕来——光看一眼，它就叫我重新变年轻了。我感激地接过来，使劲擦，小姑娘兴致浓极了，盯着这道工序，还不时指点：「好了——现在行了——右边再来一点——好——现在全掉了。」

「你确定？没剩下绿的？」我焦急地问。

「没了，现在整个儿都红了，」她欢欢喜喜地答道。「让我回家吧，」我想，被这情报搅得心烦意乱，「让我回到我那些亲爱的、从不挑剔、只会崇拜的宝贝们身边去吧，她们把我的鼻子奉为鼻子应有的典范，无论它是什么颜色都觉得美。」我把手帕塞回小姑娘手里，匆匆顺着步道走开了。她手忙脚乱地把它收起来，倒花了几秒钟，因为那手帕有一小张床单那么大；收好便跑着追上来。「你要去哪儿？」我拐上通往大门的小径时，她诧异地问。

「出这道门，」我果断地答道。

「可你不能——我们是不许从那儿走的——」

===Original===
So strong was the force of old habits in that place that at the words *not allowed* my hand dropped of itself from the latch; and at that instant a voice calling quite close to us through the mist struck me rigid.

“Elizabeth! Elizabeth!” called the voice, “Come in at once to your lessons﻿—Elizabeth! Elizabeth!”

“It’s Miss Robinson,” whispered the little girl, twinkling with excitement; then, catching sight of my face, she said once more with eager insistence, “Who are you?”

“Oh, I’m a ghost!” I cried with conviction, pressing my hands to my forehead and looking round fearfully.

“Pooh,” said the little girl.

It was the last remark I heard her make, for there was a creaking of approaching boots in the bushes, and seized by a frightful panic I pulled the gate open with one desperate pull, flung it to behind me, and fled out and away down the wide, misty fields.

The *Gotha Almanach* says that the reigning cousin married the daughter of a Mr. Johnstone, an Englishman, in 1885, and that in 1886 their only child was born, Elizabeth.

===Chinese===
旧习惯在那地方的力量竟这样大，一听「*不许*」两个字，我的手便自己从门扣上垂了下来；也正是在那一刻，一个声音穿过浓雾、就在离我们很近的地方喊起来，把我惊得僵在原地。

「伊丽莎白！伊丽莎白！」那声音喊道，「马上进来上课——伊丽莎白！伊丽莎白！」

「是罗宾逊小姐，」小姑娘悄声说，兴奋得直放光；随后瞥见我的脸色，她又急切地、不依不饶地问了一遍：「你是谁？」

「哦，我是个鬼！」我双手按住额头，惶恐地四下张望，口气斩钉截铁。

「呸，」小姑娘说。

这是我听见她说的最后一句话，因为灌木丛里已经响起渐渐逼近的、吱呀作响的靴子声；一阵可怕的恐慌攫住了我，我拼死一拽拉开大门，反手甩上，逃了出去，顺着宽阔的、雾气弥漫的田野一路逃远。

《哥达贵族年鉴》（*Gotha Almanach*）上说，在位的表亲于 1885 年娶了一位英国人约翰斯通先生的女儿，1886 年，他们唯一的孩子出世了——伊丽莎白。

## November 20th / 11月20日

===Original===
Last night we had ten degrees of frost (Fahrenheit), and I went out the first thing this morning to see what had become of the tea-roses, and behold, they were wide awake and quite cheerful﻿—covered with rime it is true, but anything but black and shrivelled. Even those in boxes on each side of the verandah steps were perfectly alive and full of buds, and one in particular, a Bouquet d’Or, is a mass of buds, and would flower if it could get the least encouragement. I am beginning to think that the tenderness of tea-roses is much exaggerated, and am certainly very glad I had the courage to try them in this northern garden. But I must not fly too boldly in the face of Providence, and have ordered those in the boxes to be taken into the greenhouse for the winter, and hope the Bouquet d’Or, in a sunny place near the glass, may be induced to open some of those buds. The greenhouse is only used as a refuge, and kept at a temperature just above freezing, and is reserved entirely for such plants as cannot stand the very coldest part of the winter out of doors. I don’t use it for growing anything, because I don’t love things that will only bear the garden for three or four months in the year and require coaxing and petting for the rest of it. Give me a garden full of strong, healthy creatures, able to stand roughness and cold without dismally giving in and dying. I never could see that delicacy of constitution is pretty, either in plants or women. No doubt there are many lovely flowers to be had by heat and constant coaxing, but then for each of these there are fifty others still lovelier that will gratefully grow in God’s wholesome air and are blessed in return with a far greater intensity of scent and colour.

===Chinese===
昨夜结了华氏十度的霜，今天一早我头一件事就是跑出去看茶香月季怎么样了，瞧啊，它们全都醒着，还挺快活——诚然蒙了一身雾凇，却绝不发黑枯萎。连游廊台阶两侧箱子里的那些也活得好好的，满是花苞，其中一株 Bouquet d’Or 更是满身花苞，只要能得到一星半点的鼓励就会开花。我开始觉得茶香月季的娇气实在被夸大了，也着实庆幸自己有胆量在这北方的花园里试种它们。但我也不可太鲁莽地拂逆天意，已经吩咐把箱子里的搬进温室过冬，希望那株 Bouquet d’Or 在玻璃近旁一个向阳的位置上，能被劝得开出几苞花来。温室只用作避难所，温度维持在冰点之上一点，专门留给那些在户外熬不过隆冬的植物。我不用它来催花育果，因为我不爱那些一年里只肯在花园待三四个月、其余时间都要人哄着宠着的东西。给我一座满是强壮健康生灵的花园吧，经得起粗放和寒冷，不会灰溜溜地认输死去。体质的娇弱算得上美——无论在植物还是女人身上——这一点我从来无法领会。毫无疑问，靠加温和不断哄劝可以弄到许多可爱的花，但每有一朵这样的花，就有五十朵更可爱的，甘愿在上帝健康的空气里生长，并蒙受回报，享有远为浓烈的芬芳与色彩。

===Original===
We have been very busy till now getting the permanent beds into order and planting the new tea-roses, and I am looking forward to next summer with more hope than ever in spite of my many failures. I wish the years would pass quickly that will bring my garden to perfection! The Persian Yellows have gone into their new quarters, and their place is occupied by the tea-rose Safrano; all the rose beds are carpeted with pansies sown in July and transplanted in October, each bed having a separate colour. The purple ones are the most charming and go well with every rose, but I have white ones with Laurette Messimy, and yellow ones with Safrano, and a new red sort in the big centre bed of red roses. Round the semicircle on the south side of the little privet hedge two rows of annual larkspurs in all their delicate shades have been sown, and just beyond the larkspurs, on the grass, is a semicircle of standard tea and pillar roses.

===Chinese===
直到现在我们都忙于把永久花床收拾停当、栽种新来的茶香月季，尽管失败累累，我对下一个夏天的盼望却胜过以往任何时候。真希望把花园带向圆满的那些年月快快过去啊！Persian Yellow 们已经搬进了新居，原来的位置换上了茶香月季 Safrano；所有玫瑰床都铺满了七月播种、十月移栽的三色堇，每床一种颜色。紫色的最迷人，配哪种玫瑰都合适；不过我给 Laurette Messimy 配了白的，给 Safrano 配了黄的，大中央那床红玫瑰里铺的是一种新的红色品种。在小女贞树篱南侧的半圆周围，播了两排一年生飞燕草，色调样样俱全；就在飞燕草再往外、草地上，是茶香月季独干树和藤本月季组成的半圆。

===Original===
In front of the house the long borders have been stocked with larkspurs, annual and perennial, columbines, giant poppies, pinks, Madonna lilies, wallflowers, hollyhocks, perennial phloxes, peonies, lavender, starworts, cornflowers, Lychnis chalcedonica, and bulbs packed in wherever bulbs could go. These are the borders that were so hardly used by the other gardener. The spring boxes for the verandah steps have been filled with pink and white and yellow tulips. I love tulips better than any other spring flower; they are the embodiment of alert cheerfulness and tidy grace, and next to a hyacinth look like a wholesome, freshly tubbed young girl beside a stout lady whose every movement weighs down the air with patchouli. Their faint, delicate scent is refinement itself; and is there anything in the world more charming than the sprightly way they hold up their little faces to the sun? I have heard them called bold and flaunting, but to me they seem modest grace itself, only always on the alert to enjoy life as much as they can and not afraid of looking the sun or anything else above them in the face. On the grass there are two beds of them carpeted with forget-me-nots; and in the grass, in scattered groups, are daffodils and narcissus. Down the wilder shrubbery walks foxgloves and mulleins will (I hope) shine majestic; and one cool corner, backed by a group of firs, is graced by Madonna lilies, white foxgloves, and columbines.

===Chinese===
房前那些长花境已经栽满了一年生和多年生的飞燕草、耧斗菜、大罂粟、石竹、圣母百合、桂竹香、蜀葵、宿根福禄考、芍药、薰衣草、紫菀、矢车菊、剪秋罗（Lychnis chalcedonica），还有球根，凡是塞得进球根的地方都塞上了。这些就是被先前那位园丁糟践得够呛的花境。游廊台阶的春箱已经装满了粉色、白色和黄色的郁金香。郁金香是我最爱胜过其他一切春花的；它们是警醒的欢快与齐整的优雅之化身，往风信子旁边一站，就像一位健健康康、刚出浴的少女站在一位胖太太身旁，后者的一举一动都让空气沉甸甸地坠着广藿香味。它们淡淡幽幽的芬芳，本身就是精致二字；而它们把小脸迎向太阳那活泼灵巧的模样，世上还有比这更迷人的吗？我听过有人说它们张扬炫耀，可在我眼里，它们分明是谦逊的优雅本身，只不过是永远警醒着，要尽情享受生活，并且不怕正视太阳或任何高于它们的东西。草地上有两床郁金香，铺着勿忘我；草地上还有成丛散植的黄水仙和水仙。在更荒野的灌木园小径两旁，毛地黄和毛蕊花将（我希望）威风凛凛地大放光彩；还有一个清凉的角落，以一丛冷杉为背景，由圣母百合、白毛地黄和耧斗菜装点着。

===Original===
In a distant glade I have made a spring garden round an oak tree that stands alone in the sun﻿—groups of crocuses, daffodils, narcissus, hyacinths, and tulips, among such flowering shrubs and trees as Pirus Malus spectabilis, floribunda, and coronaria; Prunus Juliana, Mahaleb, serotina, triloba, and Pissardi; Cydonias and Weigelias in every colour, and several kinds of Crataegus and other May lovelinesses. If the weather behaves itself nicely, and we get gentle rains in due season, I think this little corner will be beautiful﻿—but what a big “if” it is! Drought is our great enemy, and the two last summers each contained five weeks of blazing, cloudless heat when all the ditches dried up and the soil was like hot pastry. At such times the watering is naturally quite beyond the strength of two men; but as a garden is a place to be happy in, and not one where you want to meet a dozen curious eyes at every turn, I should not like to have more than these two, or rather one and a half﻿—the assistant having stork-like proclivities and going home in the autumn to his native Russia, returning in the spring with the first warm winds. I want to keep him over the winter, as there is much to be done even then, and I sounded him on the point the other day. He is the most abject-looking of human beings﻿—lame, and afflicted with a hideous eye-disease; but he is a good worker and plods along unwearyingly from sunrise to dusk.

===Chinese===
在一处远隔的林间空地上，我围绕一株独自立在阳光里的栎树造了一座春日花园——一丛丛的番红花、黄水仙、水仙、风信子和郁金香，间以种种开花的灌木与树木，如 Pirus Malus spectabilis、floribunda 与 coronaria；Prunus Juliana、Mahaleb、serotina、triloba 与 Pissardi；各色榅桲（Cydonia）与锦带花（Weigela），还有几种山楂（Crataegus）和其他五月的可爱之物。倘若天气乖乖听话，应时的好雨如期而至，我想这个小角落会很美——可这个「倘若」是多么大啊！干旱是我们的大敌，过去两个夏天，各各包含五个星期烈日炎炎、万里无云的酷热，沟渠全部干涸，土壤烫得像刚出炉的酥皮。这样的时节，浇水自然远远超出两个人的力气；但花园是一个用来快活的地方，不是一个每转个弯就要撞见一打好奇眼睛的地方，所以我不想多要人手，有两个就够，或者确切说一个半——那位助手有鹳鸟般的习性，秋天要回他俄国老家去，春天随第一阵暖风回来。我想留他过冬，因为即便那时也有许多活要干，前几天我就这件事探了探他的口风。他是人类里相貌最寒碜的——瘸腿，还害着一种可怕的眼病；但他干活是把好手，从日出到黄昏不倦地埋头苦干。

===Original===
“Pray, my good stork,” said I, or German words to that effect, “why don’t you stay here altogether, instead of going home and rioting away all you have earned?”

“I would stay,” he answered, “but I have my wife there in Russia.”

“Your wife!” I exclaimed, stupidly surprised that the poor deformed creature should have found a mate﻿—as though there were not a superfluity of mates in the world﻿—“I didn’t know you were married?”

“Yes, and I have two little children, and I don’t know what they would do if I were not to come home. But it is a very expensive journey to Russia, and costs me every time seven marks.”

“Seven marks!”

“Yes, it is a great sum.”

I wondered whether I should be able to get to Russia for seven marks, supposing I were to be seized with an unnatural craving to go there.

===Chinese===
「请问，我的好鹳鸟，」我说，或者用大意如此的德语，「你为什么不干脆留下，偏要回家去，把挣来的钱统统胡吃海喝个精光？」

「我是想留下的，」他答道，「可我老婆在俄国呢。」

「你的老婆！」我叫道，蠢头蠢脑地吃惊，这样可怜的畸形造物竟也找到了配偶——好像世上配偶还不够多似的——「我都不知道你结了婚？」

「结了，我还有两个小孩子，要是我不回家，真不知道他们可怎么办。不过回俄国一趟路费很贵，每回都要花掉我七个马克。」

「七个马克！」

「是啊，这是笔大数目。」

我心里琢磨，倘若有朝一日我被一股不合常理的瘾头攫住，非要到俄国去，七个马克够不够我到那儿。

===Original===
All the labourers who work here from March to December are Russians and Poles, or a mixture of both. We send a man over who can speak their language, to fetch as many as he can early in the year, and they arrive with their bundles, men and women and babies, and as soon as they have got here and had their fares paid, they disappear in the night if they get the chance, sometimes fifty of them at a time, to go and work singly or in couples for the peasants, who pay them a pfennig or two more a day than we do, and let them eat with the family. From us they get a mark and a half to two marks a day, and as many potatoes as they can eat. The women get less, not because they work less, but because they are women and must not be encouraged. The overseer lives with them, and has a loaded revolver in his pocket and a savage dog at his heels. For the first week or two after their arrival, the foresters and other permanent officials keep guard at night over the houses they are put into. I suppose they find it sleepy work; for certain it is that spring after spring the same thing happens, fifty of them getting away in spite of all our precautions, and we are left with our mouths open and much out of pocket. This spring, by some mistake, they arrived without their bundles, which had gone astray on the road, and, as they travel in their best clothes, they refused utterly to work until their luggage came. Nearly a week was lost waiting, to the despair of all in authority.

===Chinese===
从三月到十二月在这里做工的劳工全是俄国人和波兰人，或是两族的混血。年初我们派一个会说他们话的人过去，能带多少带多少；他们背着包袱来了，男男女女，还带着吃奶的孩子，可一到此地、路费一到手，只要逮着机会，他们就夜里消失，有时一次跑掉五十个，去给农民单干或搭伙干活——农民们一天多付一两个芬尼，还让他们上桌跟一家人一起吃饭。在我们这儿，他们一天挣一个半到两个马克，土豆管够。女工挣得更少，倒不是因为她们干得少，而是因为她们是女人，不可纵容。监工跟他们住在一起，兜里揣着上膛的左轮枪，脚跟后跟着一条凶狗。他们到达后的头一两个星期，护林员和其他正式职工夜里轮班看守他们住的房子。想必那差事叫人瞌睡；因为可以肯定的是，一个春天接一个春天，同样的事情照样发生，五十个人冲破我们万般的防范逃之夭夭，我们张着嘴干瞪眼，还赔了大笔钱。今年春天，不知怎么出了岔子，他们人到时包袱没到——包袱在路上走丢了——而他们出门穿的都是最好的衣裳，于是行李不到，他们死活不肯干活。等行李等掉了将近一个星期，让所有当官的绝望。

===Original===
Nor will any persuasions induce them to do anything on Saints’ days, and there surely never was a church so full of them as the Russian Church. In the spring, when every hour is of vital importance, the work is constantly being interrupted by them, and the workers lie sleeping in the sun the whole day, agreeably conscious that they are pleasing themselves and the Church at one and the same time﻿—a state of perfection as rare as it is desirable. Reason unaided by Faith is of course exasperated at this waste of precious time, and I confess that during the first mild days after the long winter frost when it is possible to begin to work the ground, I have sympathised with the gloom of the Man of Wrath, confronted in one week by two or three empty days on which no man will labour, and have listened in silence to his remarks about distant Russian saints.

===Chinese===
而且无论怎样劝说，都不能说动他们在圣徒节干一点活；天下也确实再没有一个教会像俄国教会那样盛产节日。春天里，每个钟头都性命攸关，活计却不断被这些节日打断，劳工们整天躺在太阳底下睡觉，心头美滋滋地意识到，他们同时取悦了自己和教会——一种既可羡又罕见的圆满境界。没有信仰扶助的理性，面对这种对宝贵时光的浪费自然恼火；我承认，在漫长冬冻之后大地初软、可以破土动工的头几天温和日子里，我是同情愤怒之人之阴郁的——一星期里撞上两三个无人肯干活的空日——也曾默默听他发表关于遥远的俄国圣徒们的高论。

===Original===
I suppose it was my own superfluous amount of civilisation that made me pity these people when first I came to live among them. They herd together like animals and do the work of animals; but in spite of the armed overseer, the dirt and the rags, the meals of potatoes washed down by weak vinegar and water, I am beginning to believe that they would strongly object to soap, I am sure they would not wear new clothes, and I hear them coming home from their work at dusk singing. They are like little children or animals in their utter inability to grasp the idea of a future; and after all, if you work all day in God’s sunshine, when evening comes you are pleasantly tired and ready for rest and not much inclined to find fault with your lot. I have not yet persuaded myself, however, that the women are happy. They have to work as hard as the men and get less for it; they have to produce offspring, quite regardless of times and seasons and the general fitness of things; they have to do this as expeditiously as possible, so that they may not unduly interrupt the work in hand; nobody helps them, notices them, or cares about them, least of all the husband. It is quite a usual thing to see them working in the fields in the morning, and working again in the afternoon, having in the interval produced a baby. The baby is left to an old woman whose duty it is to look after babies collectively. When I expressed my horror at the poor creatures working immediately afterwards as though nothing had happened, the Man of Wrath informed me that they did not suffer because they had never worn corsets, nor had their mothers and grandmothers. We were riding together at the time, and had just passed a batch of workers, and my husband was speaking to the overseer, when a woman arrived alone, and taking up a spade, began to dig. She grinned cheerfully at us as she made a curtesy, and the overseer remarked that she had just been back to the house and had a baby.

===Chinese===
我猜想，初来与这些人同住时使我怜悯他们的，正是我自己身上过剩的文明。他们像牲口一样群居，干牲口的活；可是，尽管有持枪的监工、有肮脏和褴褛、有拿稀醋水送下肚的土豆餐，我开始相信，肥皂他们会强烈抵制，新衣裳他们肯定不穿，而且我听见他们黄昏收工回家时唱着歌。他们像小孩子或小动物，全然抓不住「将来」这个概念；说到底，倘若你整天在上帝的太阳底下干活，到了晚上，你带着惬意的疲惫，正好歇息，也不大想对自己的命挑三拣四。然而，女人们是否幸福，我始终没能说服自己相信。她们得和男人干一样重的活，挣得更少；她们得生儿育女，全然不管时机季节和事情的一般妥当与否；还得尽快生完，好不过分耽误手头的活计；没有人帮她们、看她们一眼、把她们放在心上，最不放在心上的就是丈夫。早晨还看见她们在地里干活，下午又在地里干活，中间生了个孩子——这是再寻常不过的事。婴儿交给一个老太太，她的职责就是照看全体婴儿。当我表示惊骇，说这些可怜人怎么生了孩子马上就干活，仿佛没事人一样，愤怒之人便告诉我，她们并不吃苦头，因为她们从不穿束身胸衣，她们的母亲和祖母也没穿过。当时我们正并辔骑行，刚走过一伙工人，我丈夫正跟监工说话，一个女人独自走来，拿起一把铲子就挖了起来。她一面屈膝行礼，一面朝我们咧嘴快活地笑，监工说，她方才回屋去了，生了个孩子。

===Original===
“Poor, *poor* woman!” I cried, as we rode on, feeling for some occult reason very angry with the Man of Wrath. “And her wretched husband doesn’t care a rap, and will probably beat her tonight if his supper isn’t right. What nonsense it is to talk about the equality of the sexes when the women have the babies!”

“Quite so, my dear,” replied the Man of Wrath, smiling condescendingly. “You have got to the very root of the matter. Nature, while imposing this agreeable duty on the woman, weakens her and disables her for any serious competition with man. How can a person who is constantly losing a year of the best part of her life compete with a young man who never loses any time at all? He has the brute force, and his last word on any subject could always be his fist.”

===Chinese===
「可怜、*可怜*的女人！」我们继续骑行时我叫道，出于某种玄秘的原因，对愤怒之人一肚子火。「她那混账丈夫一点儿也不心疼她，今晚晚饭要是不可口，多半还要揍她。生孩子的是女人，还谈什么两性平等，多么荒唐！」

「正是如此，亲爱的，」愤怒之人答道，带着居高临下的微笑。「你已触及问题的根本。自然在把这项美差强加给女人的同时，也削弱了她，使她无力与男人进行任何认真的竞争。一个不断损失一生中最好年华里整年岁月的人，怎能竞争得过一个从不损失分毫的年轻男人？他有蛮力，无论什么题目，他的最后一句话永远可以是他的拳头。」

===Original===
I said nothing. It was a dull, gray afternoon in the beginning of November, and the leaves dropped slowly and silently at our horses’ feet as we rode towards the Hirschwald.

“It is a universal custom,” proceeded the Man of Wrath, “amongst these Russians, and I believe amongst the lower classes everywhere, and certainly commendable on the score of simplicity, to silence a woman’s objections and aspirations by knocking her down. I have heard it said that this apparently brutal action has anything but the maddening effect tenderly nurtured persons might suppose, and that the patient is soothed and satisfied with a rapidity and completeness unattainable by other and more polite methods. Do you suppose,” he went on, flicking a twig off a tree with his whip as we passed, “that the intellectual husband, wrestling intellectually with the chaotic yearnings of his intellectual wife, ever achieves the result aimed at? He may and does go on wrestling till he is tired, but never does he in the very least convince her of her folly; while his brother in the ragged coat has got through the whole business in less time than it takes me to speak about it. There is no doubt that these poor women fulfil their vocation far more thoroughly than the women in our class, and, as the truest happiness consists in finding one’s vocation quickly and continuing in it all one’s days, I consider they are to be envied rather than not, since they are early taught, by the impossibility of argument with marital muscle, the impotence of female endeavour and the blessings of content.”

===Chinese===
我什么也没说。那是十一月初一个阴沉的灰色午后，我们朝希尔施瓦尔德骑去，叶子缓缓地、无声地飘落在我们的马蹄旁。

「在俄国人中间，」愤怒之人继续说，「而且我相信在各地下层阶级中间，这是一种普遍的风俗，而且单就简捷而论确实值得称道：把女人打倒在地，好叫她的异议和抱负通通噤声。我听人说过，这一表面野蛮的举动，其效果绝非娇生惯养的人们所想象的那般令人狂怒；受术者被安抚、被满足之快之彻底，是其他更礼貌的方法所不可企及的。你以为，」我们经过时他用马鞭轻抽掉一根树枝，接着说，「那智识的丈夫，同他那智识的妻子的混沌渴望进行智识的角力，就达到过目的吗？他可以、也确实角力到精疲力竭，可从来没能哪怕一丝一毫地说服她认自己的糊涂；而他那位穿破外套的兄弟，办完全部事情所花的时间，比我谈论它的时间还短。毫无疑问，这些可怜的女人履行天职远比我们这个阶层的女人彻底；而既然最真的幸福在于迅速找到自己的天职并终身不渝，那么我认为她们毋宁说是可羡的，因为她们早早地就被教育明白了——同婚姻的肌肉讲理是不可能的——女性奋斗之无谓与知足之福祉。」

===Original===
“Pray go on,” I said politely.

“These women accept their beatings with a simplicity worthy of all praise, and far from considering themselves insulted, admire the strength and energy of the man who can administer such eloquent rebukes. In Russia, not only *may* a man beat his wife, but it is laid down in the catechism and taught all boys at the time of confirmation as necessary at least once a week, whether she has done anything or not, for the sake of her general health and happiness.”

I thought I observed a tendency in the Man of Wrath rather to gloat over these castigations.

===Chinese===
「请继续。」我客气地说。

「这些女人以一种值得一切赞美的单纯接受殴打，非但不认为受了侮辱，反而钦佩那能施行如此雄辩之训诫的男人的力量与元气。在俄国，男人不但*可以*打老婆，这事还白纸黑字写在教理问答里，在坚振礼上教给所有的男孩：为了她总的健康与幸福，至少每星期一次，不管她有没有做错什么。」

我想我察觉出，愤怒之人谈到这些惩戒时颇有几分幸灾乐祸的倾向。

===Original===
“Pray, my dear man,” I said, pointing with my whip, “look at that baby moon so innocently peeping at us over the edge of the mist just behind that silver birch; and don’t talk so much about women and things you don’t understand. What is the use of your bothering about fists and whips and muscles and all the dreadful things invented for the confusion of obstreperous wives? You know you are a civilised husband, and a civilised husband is a creature who has ceased to be a man.”

“And a civilised wife?” he asked, bringing his horse close up beside me and putting his arm round my waist, “has she ceased to be a woman?”

“I should think so indeed﻿—she is a goddess, and can never be worshipped and adored enough.”

“It seems to me,” he said, “that the conversation is growing personal.”

===Chinese===
「行了，我亲爱的先生，」我用马鞭指着说，「瞧瞧那轮婴月，多无辜地在雾霭的边上、就在那株白桦后面偷看我们；别再大谈女人和你不懂的事情了。你操心拳头、鞭子、肌肉，还有一切为收拾泼辣老婆而发明的可怕玩意儿，有什么用处呢？你知道你自己是个文明的丈夫，而文明的丈夫，是一种不再成其为男人的生物。」

「那么文明的妻子呢？」他问，把马靠到我身旁，用手臂搂住我的腰，「她也不再成其为女人了吗？」

「我看正是如此——她是一位女神，怎样崇拜怎样爱慕都不为过。」

「我看，」他说，「这谈话变得针对个人了。」

===Original===
I started off at a canter across the short, springy turf. The Hirschwald is an enchanted place on such an evening, when the mists lie low on the turf, and overhead the delicate, bare branches of the silver birches stand out clear against the soft sky, while the little moon looks down kindly on the damp November world. Where the trees thicken into a wood, the fragrance of the wet earth and rotting leaves kicked up by the horses’ hoofs fills my soul with delight. I particularly love that smell﻿—it brings before me the entire benevolence of Nature, forever working death and decay, so piteous in themselves, into the means of fresh life and glory, and sending up sweet odours as she works.

===Chinese===
我策马疾驰而去，越过短而松软的草皮。在这样的傍晚，希尔施瓦尔德是一片迷人的所在：雾霭低低地伏在草上，头顶上方，白桦细瘦的光枝在柔和的天幕前勾勒得清清楚楚，小小的月亮慈和地俯照着十一月潮湿的人间。在树木渐密成林的地方，马蹄踢起的湿土和腐叶的芬芳，把我的灵魂灌满了喜悦。我格外爱那股气味——它把自然的全副仁慈呈现在我眼前：她永远把死亡与朽坏——它们本身何其可哀——化为新生命与新荣光的养料，一边劳作，一边送出甜香。

## December 7th / 12月7日

===Original===
I have been to England. I went for at least a month and stayed a week in a fog and was blown home again in a gale. Twice I fled before the fogs into the country to see friends with gardens, but it was raining, and except the beautiful lawns (not to be had in the Fatherland) and the infinite possibilities, there was nothing to interest the intelligent and garden-loving foreigner, for the good reason that you cannot be interested in gardens under an umbrella. So I went back to the fogs, and after groping about for a few days more began to long inordinately for Germany. A terrific gale sprang up after I had started, and the journey both by sea and land was full of horrors, the trains in Germany being heated to such an extent that it is next to impossible to sit still, great gusts of hot air coming up under the cushions, the cushions themselves being very hot, and the wretched traveller still hotter.

But when I reached my home and got out of the train into the purest, brightest snow-atmosphere, the air so still that the whole world seemed to be listening, the sky cloudless, the crisp snow sparkling underfoot and on the trees, and a happy row of three beaming babies awaiting me, I was consoled for all my torments, only remembering them enough to wonder why I had gone away at all.

===Chinese===
我去了一趟英格兰。原本打算至少去一个月，结果在大雾里待了一个星期，又被一场大风刮回了家。有两回我赶在雾前逃到乡下去看望有花园的朋友，可是天总在下雨；除了美丽的草坪（这在父邦德国是求不来的）和无穷的可能性之外，再没有任何东西能吸引一个聪明的、热爱园艺的外乡人，理由很充分：人无法在雨伞底下对花园产生兴趣。于是我回到雾里，又摸索了几天之后，开始没头没脑地想念德国。我动身之后刮起了一场可怕的大风，海陆两段的旅程满是苦难：德国的火车热得叫人几乎没法安坐，一阵阵热风从坐垫底下往上灌，坐垫本身滚烫，那位可怜的旅客比坐垫更烫。

可是等我到了家，走下火车，踏入那最纯净、最明亮的雪的大气——空气静得整个世界仿佛都在凝神倾听，天空无云，脚下和树上都是晶莹闪烁的脆雪，还有一排快快活活、三个小脸放光的宝贝等着我——我受的一切罪都得了补偿，只稍稍记得它们，够用来纳闷自己当初何必出门。

===Original===
The babies each had a kitten in one hand and an elegant bouquet of pine needles and grass in the other, and what with the due presentation of the bouquets and the struggles of the kittens, the hugging and kissing was much interfered with. Kittens, bouquets, and babies were all somehow squeezed into the sleigh, and off we went with jingling bells and shrieks of delight. “Directly you comes home the fun begins,” said the May baby, sitting very close to me. “How the snow purrs!” cried the April baby, as the horses scrunched it up with their feet. The June baby sat loudly singing “The King of Love my Shepherd is,” and swinging her kitten round by its tail to emphasise the rhythm.

The house, half-buried in the snow, looked the very abode of peace, and I ran through all the rooms, eager to take possession of them again, and feeling as though I had been away forever. When I got to the library I came to a standstill﻿—ah, the dear room, what happy times I have spent in it rummaging amongst the books, making plans for my garden, building castles in the air, writing, dreaming, doing nothing! There was a big peat fire blazing half up the chimney, and the old housekeeper had put pots of flowers about, and on the writing-table was a great bunch of violets scenting the room. “Oh, how *good* it is to be home again!” I sighed in my satisfaction. The babies clung about my knees, looking up at me with eyes full of love. Outside the dazzling snow and sunshine, inside the bright room and happy faces﻿—I thought of those yellow fogs and shivered. The library is not used by the Man of Wrath; it is neutral ground where we meet in the evenings for an hour before he disappears into his own rooms﻿—a series of very smoky dens in the southeast corner of the house. It looks, I am afraid, rather too gay for an ideal library; and its colouring, white and yellow, is so cheerful as to be almost frivolous. There are white bookcases all round the walls, and there is a great fireplace, and four windows, facing full south, opening on to my most cherished bit of garden, the bit round the sundial; so that with so much colour and such a big fire and such floods of sunshine it has anything but a sober air, in spite of the venerable volumes filling the shelves. Indeed, I should never be surprised if they skipped down from their places, and, picking up their leaves, began to dance.

===Chinese===
宝贝们一只手各拎一只小猫，另一只手擎着松针和青草扎成的雅致花束；既要郑重呈献花束，小猫又要挣扎，搂抱亲吻便大受干扰。小猫、花束和宝贝们好歹全挤上了雪橇，铃铛叮当、欢呼尖叫，我们出发了。「你一回家，热闹就开始啦，」五月宝贝紧挨着我坐下，说。「雪叫马踩得多好听呀！」四月宝贝喊道——马蹄正把雪踩得咯吱作响。六月宝贝坐在那儿大声唱着「仁爱之王是我的牧者」，还拎着小猫的尾巴抡圈子打拍子。

半埋在雪里的宅子，看上去正是安宁的居所，我跑遍所有的房间，急切地想重新占有它们，只觉得仿佛离家了一辈子。走到书房门口，我站住了——啊，这亲爱的房间，我在里面度过多少快活时光：翻检书籍，为花园做计划，建造空中楼阁，写作，做梦，无所事事！一大炉泥炭火烧得半截烟囱通红，老管家婆在各处摆了一盆盆花，写字台上是一大束紫罗兰，满室生香。「哦，重新回到家，滋味多么*好*啊！」我心满意足地叹息。宝贝们攀着我的膝盖，仰起满是爱意的眼睛望我。外面是晃眼的白雪与阳光，里面是明亮的房间与欢乐的脸——我想起那些黄雾，不由得打了个寒颤。书房愤怒之人是不来的；那是一块中立地带，我们傍晚在那里会面一个钟头，随后他便遁入他自己的房间——宅子东南角一连几间烟味冲天的巢穴。恐怕它看起来过于轻快，算不得理想的书房；它的色调，白的黄的，欢快得近乎轻佻。四壁是一色的白书柜，还有一座大壁炉，四扇窗子正朝南，开向我最受珍爱的那片园子——日晷周围的那一片；于是，有这么多的颜色、这么大的炉火、这么充沛的阳光，它无论如何也算不上一间端庄持重的屋子，尽管架上排满了庄严古老的卷册。说真的，倘若它们从架上一跃而下，提起书页，翩翩起舞，我也决不会吃惊。

===Original===
With this room to live in, I can look forward with perfect equanimity to being snowed up for any time Providence thinks proper; and to go into the garden in its snowed-up state is like going into a bath of purity. The first breath on opening the door is so ineffably pure that it makes me gasp, and I feel a black and sinful object in the midst of all the spotlessness. Yesterday I sat out of doors near the sundial the whole afternoon, with the thermometer so many degrees below freezing that it will be weeks finding its way up again; but there was no wind, and beautiful sunshine, and I was well wrapped up in furs. I even had tea brought out there, to the astonishment of the menials, and sat till long after the sun had set, enjoying the frosty air. I had to drink the tea very quickly, for it showed a strong inclination to begin to freeze. After the sun had gone down the rooks came home to their nests in the garden with a great fuss and fluttering, and many hesitations and squabbles before they settled on their respective trees. They flew over my head in hundreds with a mighty swish of wings, and when they had arranged themselves comfortably, an intense hush fell upon the garden, and the house began to look like a Christmas card, with its white roof against the clear, pale green of the western sky, and lamplight shining in the windows.

I had been reading a Life of Luther, lent me by our parson, in the intervals between looking round me and being happy. He came one day with the book and begged me to read it, having discovered that my interest in Luther was not as living as it ought to be; so I took it out with me into the garden, because the dullest book takes on a certain saving grace if read out of doors, just as bread and butter, devoid of charm in the drawing-room, is ambrosia eaten under a tree. I read Luther all the afternoon with pauses for refreshing glances at the garden and the sky, and much thankfulness in my heart. His struggles with devils amazed me; and I wondered whether such a day as that, full of grace and the forgiveness of sins, never struck him as something to make him relent even towards devils. He apparently never allowed himself just to be happy. He was a wonderful man, but I am glad I was not his wife.

===Chinese===
有这样一间屋子住着，任凭老天爷高兴把大雪封门封多久，我都能心平气和地泰然处之；而走进大雪封门的花园，就像走进一场纯净的沐浴。开门后的头一口气纯净得难以言表，纯净得叫人倒抽冷气，我觉得自己像一件又黑又罪孽的东西，站在这一片洁白无瑕中间。昨天我在日晷近旁的户外坐了一整个下午，温度表降到了冰点之下那么多度，得再花几个星期才爬得回来；好在没有风，阳光又好，我裹在裘皮里，严严实实。我甚至叫人把茶端到那儿去，吓了仆人们一跳，一直坐到日落之后许久，享受着凛冽的空气。茶得赶快喝，因为它表现出强烈的结冰倾向。太阳落山之后，白嘴鸦回园中的巢来了，好一番忙乱扑腾，几多踌躇、几多口角，才各自在各自的树上安顿下来。成百上千的鸦从头顶掠过，翅膀扇出浩大的呼呼风声；等它们安顿舒坦了，一片深沉的静谧罩住花园，宅子开始变得像一张圣诞贺卡：白屋顶衬着西天澄净的淡绿，窗里灯火莹然。

我一直在读一本《路德传》，是牧师借给我的，读在四下环顾和欢喜的间隙里。有一天他携着书登门，恳请我一读，因为他发现我对路德的兴趣不够鲜活，未达应有的标准；我便把书带进了花园，因为再乏味的书，一到露天阅读便沾上几分救人的雅趣，正如面包黄油在客厅里毫无魅力，坐到树下吃便成了仙馔。我读了一下午的路德，时时停下，让眼睛在花园和天空里养一养神，心里满怀感激。他同魔鬼的搏斗叫我惊叹；我不禁想，那样一个日子——满是恩典与赦罪的日子——竟从未打动过他，让他连对魔鬼也心软一回么。他显然从不允许自己单单纯纯地快乐一场。他是位了不起的人，可我庆幸自己没做他的妻子。

===Original===
Our parson is an interesting person, and untiring in his efforts to improve himself. Both he and his wife study whenever they have a spare moment, and there is a tradition that she stirs her puddings with one hand and holds a Latin grammar in the other, the grammar, of course, getting the greater share of her attention. To most German *Hausfraus* the dinners and the puddings are of paramount importance, and they pride themselves on keeping those parts of their houses that are seen in a state of perpetual and spotless perfection, and this is exceedingly praiseworthy; but, I would humbly inquire, are there not other things even more important? And is not plain living and high thinking better than the other way about? And all too careful making of dinners and dusting of furniture takes a terrible amount of precious time, and﻿—and with shame I confess that my sympathies are all with the pudding and the grammar. It cannot be right to be the slave of one’s household gods, and I protest that if my furniture ever annoyed me by wanting to be dusted when I wanted to be doing something else, and there was no one to do the dusting for me, I would cast it all into the nearest bonfire and sit and warm my toes at the flames with great contentment, triumphantly selling my dusters to the very next pedlar who was weak enough to buy them. Parsons’ wives have to do the housework and cooking themselves, and are thus not only cooks and housemaids, but if they have children﻿—and they always do have children﻿—they are head and under nurse as well; and besides these trifling duties have a good deal to do with their fruit and vegetable garden, and everything to do with their poultry. This being so, is it not pathetic to find a young woman bravely struggling to learn languages and keep up with her husband? If I were that husband, those puddings would taste sweetest to me that were served with Latin sauce. They are both severely pious, and are forever engaged in desperate efforts to practise what they preach; than which, as we all know, nothing is more difficult. He works in his parish with the most noble self-devotion, and never loses courage, although his efforts have been several times rewarded by disgusting libels pasted up on the street-corners, thrown under doors, and even fastened to his own garden wall. The peasant hereabouts is past belief low and animal, and a sensitive, intellectual parson among them is really a pearl before swine. For years he has gone on unflinchingly, filled with the most living faith and hope and charity, and I sometimes wonder whether they are any better now in his parish than they were under his predecessor, a man who smoked and drank beer from Monday morning to Saturday night, never did a stroke of work, and often kept the scanty congregation waiting on Sunday afternoons while he finished his postprandial nap. It is discouraging enough to make most men give in, and leave the parish to get to heaven or not as it pleases; but he never seems discouraged, and goes on sacrificing the best part of his life to these people when all his tastes are literary, and all his inclinations towards the life of the student. His convictions drag him out of his little home at all hours to minister to the sick and exhort the wicked; they give him no rest, and never let him feel he has done enough; and when he comes home weary, after a day’s wrestling with his parishioners’ souls, he is confronted on his doorstep by filthy abuse pasted up on his own front door. He never speaks of these things, but how shall they be hid? Everybody here knows everything that happens before the day is over, and what we have for dinner is of far greater general interest than the most astounding political earthquake. They have a pretty, roomy cottage, and a good bit of ground adjoining the churchyard. His predecessor used to hang out his washing on the tombstones to dry, but then he was a person entirely lost to all sense of decency, and had finally to be removed, preaching a farewell sermon of a most vituperative description, and hurling invective at the Man of Wrath, who sat up in his box drinking in every word and enjoying himself thoroughly. The Man of Wrath likes novelty, and such a sermon had never been heard before. It is spoken of in the village to this day with bated breath and awful joy.

===Chinese===
我们的牧师是个有趣的人，砥砺自己从不倦怠。他和妻子一有闲暇便读书，乡间还流传着一桩美谈：她一只手搅着布丁，另一只手捧着拉丁文法书——分得较多关注的，自然是文法书。对多数德国 *Hausfrau*（家庭主妇）来说，正餐和布丁高于一切；她们引以为豪的，是让宅子里凡看得见的部分永葆无可挑剔的完美，这确实值得大大称赞；可是容我谦卑地问一句：难道没有别的事情更要紧吗？简朴度日、高尚思考，难道不比反着来强吗？再说，过分精心地烹调正餐、掸拭家具，要耗掉数量惊人的宝贵光阴，而且——而且我羞愧地承认，我的同情全在布丁和文法那一边。做家中灶神的奴隶总归不对；我敢说，倘若我的家具在我想做别的事时拿「该掸灰了」来烦我，又没人替我掸，我就把它们统统扔进最近的一堆篝火，心满意足地坐下烤脚趾，再把我的掸子得意洋洋地卖给下一个老实得肯出钱的货郎。牧师太太的家务和烹调都得亲力亲为，于是她们不但是厨娘兼女仆，倘若还有孩子——她们总是有孩子的——还兼着保姆和育婴女仆；除了这些鸡毛蒜皮的职分，她们还得照管相当一部分果蔬园，而家禽则全靠她们一手操持。事情既是这样，看见一位年轻妇人奋力挣扎着学外语、好跟上丈夫的脚步，岂不叫人心酸？我若是那位丈夫，浇着拉丁文沙司端上来的布丁，在我嘴里一定最甜。他们夫妇俩都严苛地虔诚，永远在进行拼死的努力，要言行合一——尽人皆知，天下再没有比这更难的事了。他以最高贵的忘我精神在教区里操劳，从不丧失勇气，虽然他的辛劳好几回得到的报答，是令人作呕的谤书：贴在街角，塞进门缝，甚至钉上他自己家的花园墙。这一带的农夫卑陋愚顽得令人难以置信，一位敏感而有学养的牧师落在他们中间，真是把珍珠丢在猪前。多年来他毫不退缩地坚持着，满怀最鲜活的信、望、爱；我有时纳闷，如今他教区里的人，比起他前任治下可好些了么——那位前任，从星期一早晨抽烟喝啤酒直到星期六夜里，一星半点的活也不干，星期天下午还常叫稀稀拉拉的会众干等着，等他睡完饭后的午觉。这足够叫大多数人灰心认输，任凭教区自生自灭、爱升天不升天了；可他从不显出灰心，只管把一生最好的年华献给这些人，尽管他的趣味全在文学，他的心性全向着学者的生活。他的信念不管什么时辰都把他从他那小小的家里拖出去，去看护病人、劝诫恶人；信念不给他安宁，从不容他觉得功德圆满；而当他与教民们的灵魂角力了一整天、疲惫地回到家里时，迎接他的是自家前门上贴着的污言秽语。这些事他从不提起，可又怎么瞒得住呢？这里的每一个人，在天黑之前就什么都知道了；我们家晚饭吃什么，比最惊人的政治大地震更牵动人心。他们有一座漂亮宽敞的小屋，还有一大块地皮紧挨着教堂墓地。那位前任惯于把洗的衣裳晾在墓碑上晒干，不过他本来就是个廉耻丧尽的人，最后只得被撤换走人；临行还布了一篇通篇谩骂的告别道，朝着愤怒之人破口痛詈，而愤怒之人端坐在他的包厢席里，一字不漏地听着，痛快极了。愤怒之人喜欢新鲜事，这样的布道从前闻所未闻。直到今天，村里人提起它，还屏着气，怀着又惊又喜的敬畏。

## December 22nd / 12月22日

===Original===
Up to now we have had a beautiful winter. Clear skies, frost, little wind, and, except for a sharp touch now and then, very few really cold days. My windows are gay with hyacinths and lilies of the valley; and though, as I have said, I don’t admire the smell of hyacinths in the spring when it seems wanting in youth and chastity next to that of other flowers, I am glad enough now to bury my nose in their heavy sweetness. In December one cannot afford to be fastidious; besides, one is actually less fastidious about everything in the winter. The keen air braces soul as well as body into robustness, and the food and the perfume disliked in the summer are perfectly welcome then.

===Chinese===
到目前为止，我们过了一个美丽的冬天。晴空、霜冻、难得有风，除了偶尔一阵刺骨的严寒，真正冷的日子寥寥无几。我的窗台上风信子和铃兰开得正欢；虽然正如我说过的，到了春天我并不怎么欣赏风信子的香气——跟别的花一比，它显得既不青春也不贞静——可如今我把鼻子埋进它们浓郁的甜香里，只觉得满心欢喜。到了十二月，人可挑剔不起；再说，冬天里人对一切其实都不那么挑剔了。凛冽的空气把灵魂连同身体一起锻炼得强健，夏天嫌弃的食物和香气，这时候都大受欢迎。

===Original===
I am very busy preparing for Christmas, but have often locked myself up in a room alone, shutting out my unfinished duties, to study the flower catalogues and make my lists of seeds and shrubs and trees for the spring. It is a fascinating occupation, and acquires an additional charm when you know you ought to be doing something else, that Christmas is at the door, that children and servants and farm hands depend on you for their pleasure, and that, if you don’t see to the decoration of the trees and house, and the buying of the presents, nobody else will. The hours fly by shut up with those catalogues and with Duty snarling on the other side of the door. I don’t like Duty—everything in the least disagreeable is always sure to be one’s duty. Why cannot it be my duty to make lists and plans for the dear garden? “And so it *is*,” I insisted to the Man of Wrath, when he protested against what he called wasting my time upstairs. “No,” he replied sagely; “your garden is not your duty, because it is your Pleasure.”

===Chinese===
我正忙着准备圣诞，却时常把自己独自锁进一个房间，把没做完的正事关在门外，专心研究花卉目录，开列春天要买的种子、灌木和树木的清单。这是一桩迷人的活计，而且当你明知自己本该做别的——圣诞已到了门口，孩子们、仆人们、雇农们都指着你给他们张罗快活，装饰圣诞树和屋子、采买礼物的事你不管就没人管——它便平添一分魅力。与那些目录厮守在一起、听着「责任」在门外低声咆哮，几个钟头转瞬即逝。我不喜欢「责任」——凡是多少有点讨人厌的事，准保就是一个人的责任。为亲爱的花园列清单、做规划，为什么就不能是我的责任呢？「它就是！」我向愤怒之人坚持道，那时他正抗议我干他所谓「在楼上浪费时间」的勾当。「不，」他贤明地答道，「你的花园不是你的责任，因为它是你的快乐。」

===Original===
What a comfort it is to have such wells of wisdom constantly at my disposal! Anybody can have a husband, but to few is it given to have a sage, and the combination of both is as rare as it is useful. Indeed, in its practical utility the only thing I ever saw to equal it is a sofa my neighbour has bought as a Christmas surprise for her husband, and which she showed me the last time I called there—a beautiful invention, as she explained, combining a bedstead, a sofa, and a chest of drawers, and into which you put your clothes, and on top of which you put yourself, and if anybody calls in the middle of the night and you happen to be using the drawing-room as a bedroom, you just pop the bedclothes inside, and there you are discovered sitting on your sofa and looking for all the world as though you had been expecting visitors for hours.

“Pray, does he wear pyjamas?” I inquired.

But she had never heard of pyjamas.

===Chinese===
身边常有这样一口智慧的深井听凭取用，是何等的安慰！谁都嫁得到一个丈夫，但配有圣人的寥寥无几，而两者兼得于一身，既难得又管用。说到实际效用，我平生所见唯一能与它媲美的，是我邻居买来给丈夫作圣诞惊喜的一张沙发——上次登门时她指给我看了。照她的解说，这是一件美妙的发明，集床架、沙发和五斗柜于一身：衣服塞进去，你自己躺在上面；倘若半夜有人来访，而你恰好在把客厅当卧室用，只消把被褥往里一塞，来人看见的便是你端坐沙发之上，那模样活像已经恭候访客好几个钟头了。

「请问，他穿睡衣裤吗？」我问。

可她压根儿没听说过睡衣裤。

===Original===
It takes a long time to make my spring lists. I want to have a border all yellow, every shade of yellow from fieriest orange to nearly white, and the amount of work and studying of gardening books it costs me will only be appreciated by beginners like myself. I have been weeks planning it, and it is not nearly finished. I want it to be a succession of glories from May till the frosts, and the chief feature is to be the number of “ardent marigolds”—flowers that I very tenderly love—and nasturtiums. The nasturtiums are to be of every sort and shade, and are to climb and creep and grow in bushes, and show their lovely flowers and leaves to the best advantage. Then there are to be eschscholtzias, dahlias, sunflowers, zinnias, scabiosa, portulaca, yellow violas, yellow stocks, yellow sweet-peas, yellow lupins—everything that is yellow or that has a yellow variety. The place I have chosen for it is a long, wide border in the sun, at the foot of a grassy slope crowned with lilacs and pines, and facing southeast. You go through a little pine wood, and, turning a corner, are to come suddenly upon this bit of captured morning glory. I want it to be blinding in its brightness after the dark, cool path through the wood.

===Chinese===
开我的春季清单要花很长时间。我想要一畦全黄的花境，从最炽烈的橙到近乎白的深深浅浅的黄，这要费多少工夫、啃多少园艺书，只有跟我一样的新手才能体会。我已经筹划了好几个星期，还远没有完工。我要它从五月到霜降一路辉煌相继，主角是那一丛丛「热烈的金盏花」——那是我万分温柔地爱着的花——以及旱金莲。旱金莲要各色各样、深深浅浅都有，有的攀缘，有的匍匐，有的丛生，把它们可爱的花朵和叶片展示到尽善尽美。此外还要有花菱草、大丽花、向日葵、百日菊、松虫草、半支莲、黄色堇菜、黄紫罗兰、黄色香豌豆、黄色羽扇豆——凡是黄色的，或是有黄色变种的，一概都要。我选定的位置是一长条向阳的宽花境，在一道缓坡脚下，坡顶植着丁香和松树，朝向东南。穿过一片小松林，拐过弯，便会与这一片俘获的晨光猝然相遇。我要它在你走完林中幽暗清凉的小径之后，亮得叫人睁不开眼。

===Original===
That is the idea. Depression seizes me when I reflect upon the probable difference between the idea and its realisation. I am ignorant, and the gardener is, I do believe, still more so; for he was forcing some tulips, and they have all shrivelled up and died, and he says he cannot imagine why. Besides, he is in love with the cook, and is going to marry her after Christmas, and refuses to enter into any of my plans with the enthusiasm they deserve, but sits with vacant eye dreamily chopping wood from morning till night to keep the beloved one’s kitchen fire well supplied. I cannot understand anyone preferring cooks to marigolds; those future marigolds, shadowy as they are, and whose seeds are still sleeping at the seedsman’s, have shone through my winter days like golden lamps.

I wish with all my heart I were a man, for of course the first thing I should do would be to buy a spade and go and garden, and then I should have the delight of doing everything for my flowers with my own hands and need not waste time explaining what I want done to somebody else. It is dull work giving orders and trying to describe the bright visions of one’s brain to a person who has no visions and no brain, and who thinks a yellow bed should be calceolarias edged with blue.

===Chinese===
设想便是如此。一想到设想与实现之间可能有的差距，沮丧便攫住了我。我无知，而那园丁，我确信比我更无知；他催育了几株郁金香，全都蔫头耷脑地枯死了，他说想不出为什么。再说，他正跟厨娘恋爱，打算圣诞一过就娶她，因而拒不怀着我的规划应得的热忱投入其中，只从早到晚眼神空茫、如痴如梦地劈柴，好让意中人的灶火烧得旺旺的。我无法理解怎么会有人宁要厨娘不要金盏花；那些未来的金盏花，尽管还只是幻影，种子还在种子商那里沉睡，却已像一盏盏金灯，照亮了我一个又一个冬日。

我打心底里希望自己是个男人，因为那样我头一件事便是买一把铁锹，亲自去侍弄花园，便可享受事事亲手为我的花儿操劳的快乐，不必把时间浪费在向别人解释我要做什么上。发号施令，把自己脑中明亮的幻象描述给一个既无幻象又无脑的人听，是件枯燥的差事——这人还认为黄花坛就该种蒲包花、镶一道蓝边。

===Original===
I have taken care in choosing my yellow plants to put down only those humble ones that are easily pleased and grateful for little, for my soil is by no means all that it might be, and to most plants the climate is rather trying. I feel really grateful to any flower that is sturdy and willing enough to flourish here. Pansies seem to like the place and so do sweet-peas; pinks don’t, and after much coaxing gave hardly any flowers last summer. Nearly all the roses were a success, in spite of the sandy soil, except the tea-rose Adam, which was covered with buds ready to open, when they suddenly turned brown and died, and three standard Dr. Grills which stood in a row and simply sulked. I had been very excited about Dr. Grill, his description in the catalogues being specially fascinating, and no doubt I deserved the snubbing I got. “Never be excited, my dears, about *anything*,” shall be the advice I will give the three babies when the time comes to take them out to parties, “or, if you are, don’t show it. If by nature you are volcanoes, at least be only smouldering ones. Don’t look pleased, don’t look interested, don’t, above all things, look eager. Calm indifference should be written on every feature of your faces. Never show that you like any one person, or any one thing. Be cool, languid, and reserved. If you don’t do as your mother tells you and are just gushing, frisky, young idiots, snubs will be your portion. If you do as she tells you, you’ll marry princes and live happily ever after.”

===Chinese===
挑选黄色花草时我留意只录下那些随和谦卑、给点好处就知足的，因为我的土壤实在算不得理想，对多数植物来说，这气候也相当难熬。凡是够皮实、肯在这里兴旺的花，我都真心感激。三色堇似乎喜欢这地方，香豌豆也是；石竹不喜欢，好说歹说哄了半天，去年夏天几乎没开出花来。玫瑰几乎全种成功了，虽然土壤偏沙，只有茶玫瑰 Adam 例外——它满枝花苞眼看着就要开了，却忽然发褐而死——还有三株独干的 Dr. Grill，一排站在那儿，干脆闹起了别扭。我当初对 Dr. Grill 着实兴奋过一阵，它在目录里的描述格外诱人，毫无疑问，我受的这番冷落是咎由自取。「对任何事都不要兴奋，宝贝们，」等到带她们出门赴会的年纪，我要这样忠告三个宝贝，「就算兴奋，也别露出来。你们若生来是火山，至少只做闷燃的火山。别显得高兴，别显得有兴趣，尤其万万别显得热切。冷静的无动于衷应当写在你们脸上的每一处。永远不要流露你喜欢某一个人，或某一样东西。要冷静、慵懒、矜持。不听妈妈的话、只做热情外溢毛毛躁躁的小傻瓜，冷落便是你们的份。听了妈妈的话，你们就会嫁给王子，从此幸福地生活。」

===Original===
Dr. Grill must be a German rose. In this part of the world the more you are pleased to see a person the less is he pleased to see you; whereas, if you are disagreeable, he will grow pleasant visibly, his countenance expanding into wider amiability the more your own is stiff and sour. But I was not prepared for that sort of thing in a rose, and was disgusted with Dr. Grill. He had the best place in the garden—warm, sunny, and sheltered; his holes were prepared with the tenderest care; he was given the most dainty mixture of compost, clay, and manure; he was watered assiduously all through the drought when more willing flowers got nothing; and he refused to do anything but look black and shrivel. He did not die, but neither did he live—he just existed; and at the end of the summer not one of him had a scrap more shoot or leaf than when he was first put in in April. It would have been better if he had died straight away, for then I should have known what to do; as it is, there he is still occupying the best place, wrapped up carefully for the winter, excluding kinder roses, and probably intending to repeat the same conduct next year. Well, trials are the portion of mankind, and gardeners have their share, and in any case it is better to be tried by plants than persons, seeing that with plants you know that it is you who are in the wrong, and with persons it is always the other way about—and who is there among us who has not felt the pangs of injured innocence, and known them to be grievous?

===Chinese===
Dr. Grill 准是一株德国玫瑰。在这地方，你越高兴见到一个人，他越不高兴见到你；反之，你若摆出不痛快的样子，他反倒眼见得和悦起来，你的脸色越僵硬越酸涩，他的面容便舒展出越宽厚的和蔼。可我没料到一株玫瑰也来这一套，于是对 Dr. Grill 厌烦透了。他占着园中最好的位置——温暖、向阳、有庇护；他的坑穴以最体贴的细心备好；他得到的是堆肥、黏土与粪肥调制得最精美的混合物；大旱之中更有人殷勤浇水，连更听话的花都一滴没得喝的时候也一样；可他什么都不肯干，只会黑着脸色、日渐干缩。他没有死，可也没有活——他只是存在着；到夏末，它们谁也没比四月刚下地时多出一茎一叶。他若是当场死掉倒还好些，那样我就知道该怎么办了；如今呢，他还占着最好的位置，被细心包裹起来过冬，把更知恩的玫瑰挡在外面，八成还打算来年故技重施。唉，磨难是人类的份，种花园的也有自己的一份；无论如何，受植物的磨难总比受人的好——跟植物打交道，你知道错在自己；跟人打交道，情形永远反过来——而我们当中，谁没有尝过清白受屈的痛楚，谁不知道那是何等锥心？
===Original===
I have two visitors staying with me, though I have done nothing to provoke such an infliction, and had been looking forward to a happy little Christmas alone with the Man of Wrath and the babies. Fate decreed otherwise. Quite regularly, if I look forward to anything, Fate steps in and decrees otherwise; I don’t know why it should, but it does. I had not even invited these good ladies—like greatness on the modest, they were thrust upon me. One is Irais, the sweet singer of the summer, whom I love as she deserves, but of whom I certainly thought I had seen the last for at least a year, when she wrote and asked if I would have her over Christmas, as her husband was out of sorts, and she didn’t like him in that state. Neither do I like sick husbands, so, full of sympathy, I begged her to come, and here she is. And the other is Minora.

Why I have to have Minora I don’t know, for I was not even aware of her existence a fortnight ago. Then coming down cheerfully one morning to breakfast—it was the very day after my return from England—I found a letter from an English friend, who up till then had been perfectly innocuous, asking me to befriend Minora. I read the letter aloud for the benefit of the Man of Wrath, who was eating *Spickgans*, a delicacy much sought after in these parts. “Do, my dear Elizabeth,” wrote my friend, “take some notice of the poor thing. She is studying art in Dresden, and has nowhere literally to go for Christmas. She is very ambitious and hardworking—”

===Chinese===
我家里眼下住着两位客人，虽然我没做任何招致这般磨折的事，原本还盼着同愤怒之人和三个宝贝安安静静过个快活的小圣诞。命运另有安排。只要我盼着什么，命运总会插进来另作安排，回回如此；我不知道它何以如此，但它就是这样。这两位好夫人我甚至连请都没请过——正如伟人之于谦卑者，她们是被硬塞到我头上的。一位是伊蕾斯，那位夏日甜歌手，我爱她爱得恰如其分；她来信问能不能来我这里过圣诞，说她丈夫心情欠佳，她不喜欢他那副样子，而我本来满以为至少一年之内见不着她了呢。我也不喜欢闹毛病的丈夫，于是满怀同情地恳请她来，她就来了。另一位，是米诺拉。

为什么我非得收留米诺拉，我自己也不知道，因为两星期前我甚至不知道有这个人存在。后来一天早晨，我欢欢喜喜地下楼用早餐——正是我从英格兰回来的第二天——发现一封英国朋友的来信，此人此前一直全然无害，信里求我照拂米诺拉。我为了愤怒之人的益处，把信念出声来，他当时正吃着烟熏鹅（Spickgans），本地备受追捧的美味。「千万，亲爱的伊丽莎白，」我的朋友写道，「照拂一下这可怜的人儿吧。她在德累斯顿学艺术，过圣诞真的是无处可去。她雄心勃勃，又很勤勉——」

===Original===
“Then,” interrupted the Man of Wrath, “she is not pretty. Only ugly girls work hard.”

“—and she is really very clever—”

“I do not like clever girls, they are so stupid,” again interrupted the Man of Wrath.

“—and unless some kind creature like yourself takes pity on her she will be very lonely.”

“Then let her be lonely.”

“Her mother is my oldest friend, and would be greatly distressed to think that her daughter should be alone in a foreign town at such a season.”

“I do not mind the distress of the mother.”

===Chinese===
「那么，」愤怒之人插嘴道，「她不漂亮。只有丑姑娘才勤勉。」

「——而且她真的非常聪明——」

「我不喜欢聪明的姑娘，她们蠢得很。」愤怒之人再次插嘴。

「——除非有您这样心善的人怜惜她，否则她会非常孤单。」

「那就让她孤单好了。」

「她母亲是我最老的朋友，若知道女儿在这样的时节独自待在一座外国城镇里，会伤心得很。」

「那位母亲的伤心，我无所谓。」

===Original===
“Oh, dear me,” I exclaimed impatiently, “I shall have to ask her to come!”

“If you *should* be inclined,” the letter went on, “to play the good Samaritan, dear Elizabeth, I am positive you would find Minora a bright, intelligent companion—”

“Minora?” questioned the Man of Wrath.

The April baby, who has had a nursery governess of an altogether alarmingly zealous type attached to her person for the last six weeks, looked up from her bread and milk.

“It sounds like islands,” she remarked pensively.

The governess coughed.

“Majora, Minora, Alderney, and Sark,” explained her pupil.

===Chinese===
「哎呀，天哪，」我不耐烦地叫道，「看来我只好请她来了！」

「倘若您当真有意，」信接着写道，「做个好撒玛利亚人，亲爱的伊丽莎白，我敢肯定您会发现米诺拉是个明朗聪慧的伴儿——」

「米诺拉？」愤怒之人疑问道。

四月宝贝——过去六个星期里她身边一直挂着一位热心得简直吓人的幼儿家庭教师——从她的牛奶泡面包上抬起头来。

「听着像一串岛名。」她若有所思地说。

家庭教师咳嗽了一声。

「马约卡、米诺拉、奥尔德尼，还有萨克。」她的学生解释道。

===Original===
I looked at her severely.

“If you are not careful, April,” I said, “you’ll be a genius when you grow up and disgrace your parents.”

Miss Jones looked as though she did not like Germans. I am afraid she despises us because she thinks we are foreigners—an attitude of mind quite British and wholly to her credit; but we, on the other hand, regard *her* as a foreigner, which, of course, makes things complicated.

“Shall I really have to have this strange girl?” I asked, addressing nobody in particular and not expecting a reply.

===Chinese===
我严厉地看了她一眼。

「小心点儿，四月，」我说，「再不长心，你长大了要成天才，把爹娘的脸都丢尽。」

琼斯小姐的神情像是不喜欢德国人。恐怕她瞧不起我们，因为她觉得我们是外国人——这种心态十足英国做派，全然是她的加分项；可我们呢，反过来又把「她」当作外国人，这下事情当然就复杂了。

「我真非得接待这个陌生姑娘不可吗？」我问道，不特地对谁说，也不指望有人回答。

===Original===
“You need not have her,” said the Man of Wrath composedly, “but you will. You will write today and cordially invite her, and when she has been here twenty-four hours you will quarrel with her. I know you, my dear.”

“Quarrel! I? With a little art-student?”

===Chinese===
「你不必接待她，」愤怒之人从容地说，「但你会的。你今天就会写信热诚地邀请她，等她到这里满了二十四小时，你就会跟她吵起来。我了解你，亲爱的。」

「吵架！我？跟一个学艺术的小姑娘？」

===Original===
Miss Jones cast down her eyes. She is perpetually scenting a scene, and is always ready to bring whole batteries of discretion and tact and good taste to bear on us, and seems to know we are disputing in an unseemly manner when we would never dream it ourselves but for the warning of her downcast eyes. I would take my courage in both hands and ask her to go, for besides this superfluity of discreet behaviour she is, although only nursery, much too zealous, and inclined to be always teaching and never playing; but, unfortunately, the April baby adores her and is sure there never was anyone so beautiful before. She comes every day with fresh accounts of the splendours of her wardrobe, and feeling descriptions of her umbrellas and hats; and Miss Jones looks offended and purses up her lips. In common with most governesses, she has a little dark down on her upper lip, and the April baby appeared one day at dinner with her own decorated in faithful imitation, having achieved it after much struggling, with the aid of a lead pencil and unbounded love. Miss Jones put her in the corner for impertinence. I wonder why governesses are so unpleasant. The Man of Wrath says it is because they are not married. Without venturing to differ entirely from the opinion of experience, I would add that the strain of continually having to set an example must surely be very great. It is much easier, and often more pleasant, to be a warning than an example, and governesses are but women, and women are sometimes foolish, and when you want to be foolish it must be annoying to have to be wise.

===Chinese===
琼斯小姐垂下了眼睛。她永远在嗅探一场风波的气味，随时准备把整整几排炮的审慎、机变和雅趣对准我们；若不是她垂眼示警，我们自己做梦也想不到竟在失态地争执。我真想鼓起勇气请她走人，因为除了这份过分周到的做派，她虽只是幼儿教师，却热心得过了头，一味好为人师、从不肯陪着玩；可惜四月宝贝崇拜她，认定天底下再没有过这么美的人。她每天都来报告她衣柜里种种华服的新鲜消息，还深情地描述她的阳伞和帽子；琼斯小姐便面露愠色，抿紧嘴唇。和多数家庭女教师一样，她上唇有一层淡淡的汗毛，有一天四月宝贝也在自己上唇如法炮制了一道出现在饭桌前——那是她费了好大的劲、借一枝铅笔和无限的爱慕描画出来的。琼斯小姐罚她坐墙角，罪名是无礼。我奇怪家庭女教师们为什么都这么惹人厌。愤怒之人说，那是因为她们没嫁人。我不敢全然违逆经验之谈，只想补充一句：时时处处要给人做榜样，这份劳神必定很折磨人。做一面警示总比做一个榜样容易，也常常更愉快；家庭女教师终究也是女人，女人有时也想犯犯傻，而你想犯傻的时候偏要装贤明，必定恼人。
===Original===
Minora and Irais arrived yesterday together; or rather, when the carriage drove up, Irais got out of it alone, and informed me that there was a strange girl on a bicycle a little way behind. I sent back the carriage to pick her up, for it was dusk and the roads are terrible.

“But why do you have strange girls here at all?” asked Irais rather peevishly, taking off her hat in the library before the fire, and otherwise making herself very much at home; “I don’t like them. I’m not sure that they’re not worse than husbands who are out of order. Who is she? She would bicycle from the station, and is, I am sure, the first woman who has done it. The little boys threw stones at her.”

“Oh, my dear, that only shows the ignorance of the little boys. Never mind her. Let us have tea in peace before she comes.”

“But we should be much happier without her,” she grumbled. “Weren’t we happy enough in the summer, Elizabeth—just you and I?”

“Yes, indeed we were,” I answered heartily, putting my arms round her. The flame of my affection for Irais burns very brightly on the day of her arrival; besides, this time I have prudently provided against her sinning with the saltcellars by ordering them to be handed round like vegetable dishes. We had finished tea and she had gone up to her room to dress before Minora and her bicycle were got here. I hurried out to meet her, feeling sorry for her, plunged into a circle of strangers at such a very personal season as Christmas. But she was not very shy; indeed, she was less shy than I was, and lingered in the hall, giving the servants directions to wipe the snow off the tyres of her machine before she lent an attentive ear to my welcoming remarks.

===Chinese===
米诺拉和伊蕾斯昨天一道到达；更确切地说，马车驶到门前时，只有伊蕾斯一个人下了车，告诉我不远处后面有个骑自行车的陌生姑娘。我打发马车回头去接她，因为天已擦黑，路面又糟得厉害。

「可你到底为什么要让陌生姑娘进门？」伊蕾斯相当烦躁地问。她在书房炉火前摘下帽子，旁的事上也一副宾至如归的做派；「我不喜欢她们。我说不好她们是不是比出了毛病的丈夫还要糟。她是谁？她非要从车站骑车来，而且我敢肯定，她是头一个这么干的女人。小男孩们朝她扔石头来着。」

「噢，亲爱的，那只说明小男孩们没见识。别管她。趁她没到，咱们先安安静静喝个茶。」

「可是没有她我们会快活得多，」她嘟囔道，「今年夏天我们不就够快活的吗，伊丽莎白——就你我两个？」

「是啊，可不是嘛。」我衷心地答道，伸臂搂住她。我对伊蕾斯的爱火，在她到达的那一天烧得最旺；再说，这一回我已审慎地防备了她在盐罐上犯罪——吩咐让盐罐像蔬菜碟一样挨个传着上。茶用完，她上楼更衣去了，米诺拉和她的自行车才被接到了。我赶忙出去迎她，心里替她难过：圣诞这样最讲私谊的时节，她却被投进一圈陌生人当中。可她并不怎么怯生；说真的，她比我还不怯生，先在门厅里流连，指挥仆人们把她车胎上的雪擦干净，然后才肯专注地聆听我的欢迎辞。

===Original===
“I couldn’t make your man understand me at the station,” she said at last, when her mind was at rest about her bicycle; “I asked him how far it was, and what the roads were like, and he only smiled. Is he German? But of course he is—how odd that he didn’t understand. You speak English very well—very well indeed, do you know.” By this time we were in the library, and she stood on the hearthrug warming her back while I poured her out some tea.

“What a quaint room,” she remarked, looking round, “and the hall is so curious too. Very old, isn’t it? There’s a lot of copy here.”

The Man of Wrath, who had been in the hall on her arrival and had come in with us, began to look about on the carpet. “Copy?” he inquired, “Where’s copy?”

“Oh—material, you know, for a book. I’m just jotting down what strikes me in your country, and when I have time shall throw it into book form.” She spoke very loud, as English people always do to foreigners.

===Chinese===
「在车站，我实在没法让你们那位男仆听懂我的话，」待自行车的悬心放下之后，她终于开口道；「我问他有多远、路怎么样，他只是一个劲儿笑。他是德国人吧？那当然了——真怪，他居然听不懂。您英语说得真好——真好，您知道吗。」这时我们已进了书房，她站在炉前地毯上烘着后背，我给她斟茶。

「多古雅的房间，」她环顾四周，评点道，「那门厅也真有意思。很老了吧？这里可有的是素材。」

愤怒之人——她到达时他在门厅里，同我们一起进来——开始在地板上四下张望。「素材？」他问道，「素材在哪里？」

「哦——就是材料，您知道的，写书用的。我只是随手记下你们国家让我觉得新鲜的东西，等有了工夫，就把它整理成书。」她说得非常响，英国人对老外说话向来如此。

===Original===
“My dear,” I said breathlessly to Irais, when I had got into her room and shut the door and Minora was safely in hers, “what do you think—she writes books!”

“What—the bicycling girl?”

“Yes—Minora—imagine it!”

We stood and looked at each other with awestruck faces.

“How dreadful!” murmured Irais. “I never met a young girl who did that before.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的，」等我进了伊蕾斯的房间、关上门、米诺拉也安稳进了她自己那间之后，我气都喘不匀地对她说，「你猜怎么着——她写书！」

「什么——那个骑车的姑娘？」

「是啊——米诺拉——你想想！」

我们站着，面面相觑，满脸敬畏。

「可怕极了！」伊蕾斯喃喃道。「这样的小姑娘，我从前可从来没遇到过。」

===Original===
“She says this place is full of copy.”

“Full of what?”

“That’s what you make books with.”

“Oh, my dear, this is worse than I expected! A strange girl is always a bore among good friends, but one can generally manage her. But a girl who writes books—why, it isn’t respectable! And you can’t snub that sort of people; they’re unsnubbable.”

“Oh, but we’ll try!” I cried, with such heartiness that we both laughed.

===Chinese===
「她说这地方满处都是素材。」

「满处都是什么？」

「就是拿来写书的东西。」

「噢，亲爱的，这比我预想的还糟！好朋友中间插进个陌生姑娘，本来就够烦人的了，不过通常还对付得了。可一个写书的姑娘——天哪，这不成体统！而且这种人你冷落不得；他们冷落不动。」

「噢，咱们试试看嘛！」我喊道，喊得那么起劲，我们俩都笑了。

===Original===
The hall and the library struck Minora most; indeed, she lingered so long after dinner in the hall, which is cold, that the Man of Wrath put on his fur coat by way of a gentle hint. His hints are always gentle.

She wanted to hear the whole story about the chapel and the nuns and Gustavus Adolphus, and pulling out a fat notebook began to take down what I said. I at once relapsed into silence.

“Well?” she said.

“That’s all.”

“Oh, but you’ve only just begun.”

“It doesn’t go any further. Won’t you come into the library?”

In the library she again took up her stand before the fire and warmed herself, and we sat in a row and were cold. She has a wonderfully good profile, which is irritating. The wind, however, is tempered to the shorn lamb by her eyes being set too closely together.

===Chinese===
门厅和书房给米诺拉的印象最深；的确，晚饭后她在冷冰冰的门厅里流连得太久，愤怒之人便穿上了他的皮大衣，算是委婉的提醒。他的提醒向来委婉。

她想听关于小教堂、修女和古斯塔夫·阿道夫的完整故事，还掏出一本厚厚的笔记本，开始记录我说的话。我立刻重新陷入沉默。

「后来呢？」她说。

「就这些。」

「哦，可您才刚开了个头。」

「再往下就没有了。到书房来坐坐好吗？」

到了书房，她又径自在炉前站定，烘着火，我们则坐成一排挨冻。她的侧脸出奇地好看，这很叫人来气。不过，天意总算怜惜剪了毛的羊——她那两只眼睛挤得太近了。

===Original===
Irais lit a cigarette, and leaning back in her chair, contemplated her critically beneath her long eyelashes. “You are writing a book?” she asked presently.

“Well—yes, I suppose I may say that I am. Just my impressions, you know, of your country. Anything that strikes me as curious or amusing—I jot it down, and when I have time shall work it up into something, I daresay.”

“Are you not studying painting?”

“Yes, but I can’t study that forever. We have an English proverb: ‘Life is short and Art is long’—too long, I sometimes think—and writing is a great relaxation when I am tired.”

“What shall you call it?”

“Oh, I thought of calling it *Journeyings in Germany*. It sounds well, and would be correct. Or *Jottings from German Journeyings*—I haven’t quite decided yet which.”

===Chinese===
伊蕾斯点起一支烟，靠在椅背上，从长长的睫毛底下挑剔地端详她。「你在写书？」过了一会儿她问。

「嗯——是吧，我想可以这么说。就是我对你们国家的印象，您知道的。凡是让我觉得新奇或有趣的东西——我就随手记下来，等有了工夫，想必会把它写成点什么。」

「你不是在学画吗？」

「是啊，可绘画不能永远学下去呀。我们有句英国谚语：『人生苦短，艺术久长』——有时我觉得实在太长了——写写字，累的时候是很好的消遣。」

「你打算给它起个什么书名？」

「哦，我想过叫《德国之行》（Journeyings in Germany）。听着不错，也切题。或者《德国之行拾零》（Jottings from German Journeyings）——两个里头挑哪个，我还没拿定主意。」
===Original===
“By the author of *Prowls in Pomerania*, you might add,” suggested Irais.

“And *Drivel from Dresden*,” said I.

“And *Bosh from Berlin*,” added Irais.

Minora stared. “I don’t think those two last ones would do,” she said, “because it is not to be a facetious book. But your first one is rather a good title,” she added, looking at Irais and drawing out her notebook. “I think I’ll just jot that down.”

“If you jot down all we say and then publish it, will it still be your book?” asked Irais.

But Minora was so busy scribbling that she did not hear.

===Chinese===
「不妨再添一句：作者是《波美拉尼亚漫游》（Prowls in Pomerania）的作者。」伊蕾斯提议道。

「还有《德累斯顿呓语》（Drivel from Dresden）。」我说。

「还有《柏林胡话》（Bosh from Berlin）。」伊蕾斯补充道。

米诺拉瞪大了眼。「我看后两个不合适，」她说，「因为这本书不打算写成滑稽书。不过您头一个倒是挺不错的书名，」她望着伊蕾斯，又补上一句，一面掏出笔记本，「我想就把它记下来。」

「要是把我们说的话统统记下来再拿去出版，那还算你的书吗？」伊蕾斯问。

可米诺拉只顾埋头疾书，没有听见。

===Original===
“And have *you* no suggestions to make, Sage?” asked Irais, turning to the Man of Wrath, who was blowing out clouds of smoke in silence.

“Oh, do you call him Sage?” cried Minora; “and always in English?”

Irais and I looked at each other. We knew what we did call him, and were afraid Minora would in time ferret it out and enter it in her notebook. The Man of Wrath looked none too well pleased to be alluded to under his very nose by our new guest as “him.”

“Husbands are always sages,” said I gravely.

“Though sages are not always husbands,” said Irais with equal gravity. “Sages and husbands—sage and husbands—” she went on musingly, “what does that remind you of, Miss Minora?”

“Oh, I know—how stupid of me!” cried Minora eagerly, her pencil in midair and her brain clutching at the elusive recollection, “sage and—why—yes—no—yes, of course—oh,” disappointedly, “but that’s vulgar—I can’t put it in.”

===Chinese===
「那么「您」没有什么建议要提吗，圣人？」伊蕾斯转向愤怒之人问道，他正默不作声地喷云吐雾。

「哦，你们叫他圣人？」米诺拉叫道；「而且一直用英语这么叫？」

我和伊蕾斯互望了一眼。我们知道自己背地里叫他什么，生怕米诺拉早晚要把它嗅出来，记进她的笔记本。新来的客人当着愤怒之人的面、就在他鼻子底下用一个「他」字相称，他看上去不怎么痛快。

「丈夫个个都是圣人。」我一本正经地说。

「不过圣人不一定个个是丈夫。」伊蕾斯同样一本正经地说。「圣人与丈夫们——鼠尾草与丈夫们——」她若有所思地念叨下去，「米诺拉小姐，这让你想起什么了？」

「哦，我知道了——我真笨！」米诺拉急切地喊道，铅笔悬在半空，脑子里正抓向那段躲躲闪闪的回忆，「鼠尾草和——咦——嗯——不——嗯——对，当然是——哦，」她失望地说，「可那个太不雅了——我不能写进去。」

===Original===
“What is vulgar?” I asked.

“She thinks sage and onions is vulgar,” said Irais languidly; “but it isn’t, it is very good.” She got up and walked to the piano, and, sitting down, began, after a little wandering over the keys, to sing.

“Do you play?” I asked Minora.

“Yes, but I am afraid I am rather out of practice.”

I said no more. I know what *that* sort of playing is.

===Chinese===
「什么太不雅？」我问。

「她觉得鼠尾草拌洋葱不体面，」伊蕾斯懒洋洋地说，「才不呢，那东西好得很。」她起身走到钢琴边，坐下，手指在琴键上略一流连，便唱了起来。

「你弹琴吗？」我问米诺拉。

「弹，只怕是荒疏得厉害了。」

我没再说什么。「那」种弹法是什么光景，我可领教过。

===Original===
When we were lighting our bedroom candles Minora began suddenly to speak in an unknown tongue. We stared. “What is the matter with her?” murmured Irais.

“I thought, perhaps,” said Minora in English, “you might prefer to talk German, and as it is all the same to me what I talk—”

“Oh, pray don’t trouble,” said Irais. “We like airing our English—don’t we, Elizabeth?”

“I don’t want my German to get rusty though,” said Minora; “I shouldn’t like to forget it.”

“Oh, but isn’t there an English song,” said Irais, twisting round her neck as she preceded us upstairs, “‘’Tis folly to remember, ’tis wisdom to forget’?”

===Chinese===
到了点卧室蜡烛的时候，米诺拉忽然说起一种未知的语言来。我们愣住了。「她这是怎么了？」伊蕾斯低声道。

「我想，也许，」米诺拉改用英语说，「你们更愿意说德语，反正我说什么都无所谓——」

「噢，千万别费心，」伊蕾斯说，「我们正喜欢晾晾英语——不是吗，伊丽莎白？」

「不过我不想把德语荒疏了，」米诺拉说，「我不愿意把它忘掉。」

「哦，可英语歌里不是有这么一句吗，」伊蕾斯走在前头领我们上楼，脖子拧过来对我们说，「『记住是痴愚，忘却才是智慧』？」

===Original===
“You are not nervous sleeping alone, I hope,” I said hastily.

“What room is she in?” asked Irais.

“No. 12.”

“Oh!—do you believe in ghosts?”

Minora turned pale.

“What nonsense,” said I; “we have no ghosts here. Good night. If you want anything, mind you ring.”

“And if you see anything curious in that room,” called Irais from her bedroom door, “mind you jot it down.”

===Chinese===
「你一个人睡不心慌吧，希望。」我急忙转了话头。

「她住哪间房？」伊蕾斯问。

「12 号。」

「哦！——你信鬼吗？」

米诺拉脸都白了。

「胡说什么，」我说，「我们这里没有鬼。晚安。缺什么的话，记得按铃。」

「要是你在那间房里看见什么希奇的东西，」伊蕾斯从她卧室门口喊道，「记得记下来啊。」

## December 27th / 12月27日

===Original===
It is the fashion, I believe, to regard Christmas as a bore of rather a gross description, and as a time when you are invited to overeat yourself, and pretend to be merry without just cause. As a matter of fact, it is one of the prettiest and most poetic institutions possible, if observed in the proper manner, and after having been more or less unpleasant to everybody for a whole year, it is a blessing to be forced on that one day to be amiable, and it is certainly delightful to be able to give presents without being haunted by the conviction that you are spoiling the recipient, and will suffer for it afterward. Servants are only big children, and are made just as happy as children by little presents and nice things to eat, and, for days beforehand, every time the three babies go into the garden they expect to meet the Christ Child with His arms full of gifts. They firmly believe that it is thus their presents are brought, and it is such a charming idea that Christmas would be worth celebrating for its sake alone.

===Chinese===
我相信，时下流行的看法是把圣诞节看作一桩相当粗鄙的闷事，一个请你暴饮暴食、无缘无故装出欢乐的时节。事实上，只要过得得法，它是可能有的最美丽、最有诗意的制度之一；对所有人不冷不热地别扭了一整年之后，能有一天被迫和颜悦色，实在是一种福分；而且，送礼物时不必被「你这是在惯坏收礼的人、日后要吃苦头」的念头纠缠，也着实令人愉快。仆人不过是大孩子，小小的礼物和好吃的就能让他们像孩子一样开心；而节前好些天，三个宝贝每次走进花园，都盼着遇见怀抱满捧礼物的圣婴。她们坚信礼物就是这样送来的，这念头太迷人了，单为它就值得庆祝圣诞。

===Original===
As great secrecy is observed, the preparations devolve entirely on me, and it is not very easy work, with so many people in our own house and on each of the farms, and all the children, big and little, expecting their share of happiness. The library is uninhabitable for several days before and after, as it is there that we have the trees and presents. All down one side are the trees, and the other three sides are lined with tables, a separate one for each person in the house. When the trees are lighted, and stand in their radiance shining down on the happy faces, I forget all the trouble it has been, and the number of times I have had to run up and down stairs, and the various aches in head and feet, and enjoy myself as much as anybody. First the June baby is ushered in, then the others and ourselves according to age, then the servants, then come the head inspector and his family, the other inspectors from the different farms, the mamsells, the bookkeepers and secretaries, and then all the children, troops and troops of them﻿—the big ones leading the little ones by the hand and carrying the babies in their arms, and the mothers peeping round the door. As many as can get in stand in front of the trees, and sing two or three carols; then they are given their presents, and go off triumphantly, making room for the next batch. My three babies sang lustily too, whether they happened to know what was being sung or not. They had on white dresses in honour of the occasion, and the June baby was even arrayed in a low-necked and short-sleeved garment, after the manner of Teutonic infants, whatever the state of the thermometer. Her arms are like miniature prizefighter’s arms﻿—I never saw such things; they are the pride and joy of her little nurse, who had tied them up with blue ribbons, and kept on kissing them. I shall certainly not be able to take her to balls when she grows up, if she goes on having arms like that.

===Chinese===
因为一切都在极度保密中进行，筹备工作便全落在我一个人身上，这活儿可不太轻松：我们自己家里、各个农场上人有这么多，大大小小的孩子都盼着各得一份幸福。前后好几天书房都没法住人，因为圣诞树和礼物都设在那里。一侧墙边排满圣诞树，另外三面排满桌子，家里每人单独一张。当圣诞树点亮了，立在光辉里，照着一张张快乐的脸，我便忘掉了这一路的所有操劳，忘掉我上下楼梯跑了多少趟，忘掉头上脚上的种种酸痛，和别人一样尽情快活。最先被领进来的是六月宝贝，然后按年龄依次是其余两个和我们自己，然后是仆人们，然后是总管一家、各农场来的管事们、女管家们、账房和文书们，最后是所有的孩子，一队接一队——大的牵着小的手，怀里还抱着吃奶的，母亲们在门边探头。能挤进来的人都站到圣诞树前，唱两三首圣诞颂歌；然后领了礼物，凯旋而去，把地方让给下一批。我的三个宝贝也放声大唱，不管唱的究竟会不会。她们为着这个日子穿上了白裙子，六月宝贝更是照条顿婴儿的做派，穿上了低领短袖的衣裳，全不管温度计怎么说。她的两条胳膊像微缩的职业拳师的胳膊——我从没见过那样的东西；她的小保姆引以为豪、视如至宝，用蓝缎带把它们扎起来，还不停地亲。她要是胳膊一直这样长下去，等她长大，我肯定没法带她去舞会。

===Original===
When they came to say good night, they were all very pale and subdued. The April baby had an exhausted-looking Japanese doll with her, which she said she was taking to bed, not because she liked him, but because she was so sorry for him, he seemed so very tired. They kissed me absently, and went away, only the April baby glancing at the trees as she passed and making them a curtesy.

“Goodbye, trees,” I heard her say; and then she made the Japanese doll bow to them, which he did, in a very languid and blasé fashion. “*You’ll* never see such trees again,” she told him, giving him a vindictive shake, “for you’ll be brokened *long* before next time.”

She went out, but came back as though she had forgotten something.

“Thank the *Christkind* so *much*, Mummy, won’t you, for all the lovely things He brought us. I suppose you’re writing to Him now, isn’t you?”

===Chinese===
她们来道晚安时，个个脸色苍白，安安静静。四月宝贝带着一个一脸倦容的日本娃娃，说要把他带上床，不是因为她喜欢他，而是因为她太心疼他了，他看上去累极了。她们心不在焉地亲了亲我，走开了，只有四月宝贝经过圣诞树时望了它们一眼，向它们行了个屈膝礼。

「再见了，树儿们。」我听见她说；然后她让日本娃娃向树们鞠躬，他也照办了，一副十分倦怠、见惯不惊的架势。「*你*再也见不到这样的树了，」她对他说，狠狠摇了他一下，「因为你到不了下回，早就被摔碎碎啦。」

她走了出去，又折了回来，仿佛忘了什么似的。

「替我好好谢谢*Christkind*（圣婴）好吗，妈妈，谢谢他带给我们的所有好东西。我想你这会儿正在给他写信吧，是不是你？」

===Original===
I cannot see that there was anything gross about our Christmas, and we were perfectly merry without any need to pretend, and for at least two days it brought us a little nearer together, and made us kind. Happiness is so wholesome; it invigorates and warms me into piety far more effectually than any amount of trials and griefs, and an unexpected pleasure is the surest means of bringing me to my knees. In spite of the protestations of some peculiarly constructed persons that they are the better for trials, I don’t believe it. Such things must sour us, just as happiness must sweeten us, and make us kinder, and more gentle. And will anybody affirm that it behoves us to be more thankful for trials than for blessings? We were meant to be happy, and to accept all the happiness offered with thankfulness﻿—indeed, we are none of us ever thankful enough, and yet we each get so much, so very much, more than we deserve. I know a woman﻿—she stayed with me last summer﻿—who rejoices grimly when those she loves suffer. She believes that it is our lot, and that it braces us and does us good, and she would shield no one from even unnecessary pain; she weeps with the sufferer, but is convinced it is all for the best. Well, let her continue in her dreary beliefs; she has no garden to teach her the beauty and the happiness of holiness, nor does she in the least desire to possess one; her convictions have the sad gray colouring of the dingy streets and houses she lives amongst﻿—the sad colour of humanity in masses. Submission to what people call their “lot” is simply ignoble. If your lot makes you cry and be wretched, get rid of it and take another; strike out for yourself; don’t listen to the shrieks of your relations, to their gibes or their entreaties; don’t let your own microscopic set prescribe your goings-out and comings-in; don’t be afraid of public opinion in the shape of the neighbour in the next house, when all the world is before you new and shining, and everything is possible, if you will only be energetic and independent and seize opportunity by the scruff of the neck.

===Chinese===
我看不出我们的圣诞节有什么粗鄙可言，我们的欢乐货真价实，无须假装；而且至少有两天，它让我们彼此靠近了一点，让我们变得和善。幸福是这样有益健康；它滋养我、温暖我，使我生出虔敬之心，远比任何数量的磨难和悲哀都更管用，而一场不期而至的喜悦，是把我们引到膝头祷告的最可靠的法子。尽管有些构造特殊的人士坚称磨难于他们有益，我不信。磨难必定让我们变酸，正如幸福必定让我们变甜，让我们更善良、更温柔。可会有人断言，我们理应对磨难比对恩典更感恩吗？我们生来就是要幸福的，就该满怀感谢地领受献上来的一切幸福——说真的，我们谁也感谢得不够，而我们每个人得到的，都远远、远远超过我们应得的。我认识一位女人——去年夏天她在我这儿住过——她所爱的人受苦时，她会铁青着脸欢欣。她相信受苦是我们的命，相信它磨砺我们、对我们有好处；哪怕是不必要的痛苦，她也决不给任何人挡一挡；她陪着受苦的人流泪，心里却坚信这一切都是最好的安排。好吧，就让她守着她那些阴郁的信条吧；她没有一座花园来教她圣洁之美与圣洁之福，她也丝毫不想拥有一座；她的信念带着她所生活的那些昏暗街道和房屋的悲哀的灰色——人成群结队时的悲哀的颜色。顺从于人们所谓的「命」，简直是卑怯。倘若你的命让你哭泣、让你可怜，就甩掉它，另换一个；自己去闯；别听你亲戚们的尖叫，别理他们的讥讽或哀求；别让你那显微镜般的小圈子规定你的进出；别害怕以隔壁邻居面目出现的公众舆论——当整个世界崭新而闪亮地在你面前展开，只要你肯干、肯独立、肯一把揪住机会的后颈皮，一切皆有可能。

===Original===
“To hear you talk,” said Irais, “no one would ever imagine that you dream away your days in a garden with a book, and that you never in your life seized anything by the scruff of its neck. And what is scruff? I hope I have not got any on me.” And she craned her neck before the glass.

She and Minora were going to help me decorate the trees, but very soon Irais wandered off to the piano, and Minora was tired and took up a book; so I called in Miss Jones and the babies﻿—it was Miss Jones’s last public appearance, as I shall relate﻿—and after working for the best part of two days they were finished, and looked like lovely ladies in widespreading, sparkling petticoats, holding up their skirts with glittering fingers. Minora wrote a long description of them for a chapter of her book which is headed “Noel”﻿—I saw that much, because she left it open on the table while she went to talk to Miss Jones. They were fast friends from the very first, and though it is said to be natural to take to one’s own countrymen, I am unable altogether to sympathise with such a reason for sudden affection.

===Chinese===
「听你说话的口气，」伊蕾斯说，「谁也想不到你整天拿着一本书在花园里做梦，也想不到你一辈子从没揪住过任何东西的后颈皮。话说回来，后颈皮是什么？但愿我身上没长这个。」说着她在镜子前伸长了脖子。

她和米诺拉本打算帮我装饰圣诞树，可是不一会儿伊蕾斯就晃到钢琴边去了，米诺拉累了，拿起一本书；于是我唤来了琼斯小姐和宝贝们——这是琼斯小姐最后一次公开露面，这个我后面再交代——苦干了差不多两天，圣诞树总算完工，看上去像一群可爱的女士，穿着闪闪发光的宽大衬裙，用亮晶晶的手指提着裙裾。米诺拉为它们写了一大段描写，收进她书里题为《Noel》（圣诞）的一章——我看到的就是这么多，因为她去和琼斯小姐说话时，把它摊开在桌上。她们俩一见如故，成了铁杆朋友；虽说亲附自己的同胞乃人之常情，可对这种突如其来的感情爆发的原因，我实在无法全然苟同。

===Original===
“I wonder what they talk about?” I said to Irais yesterday, when there was no getting Minora to come to tea, so deeply was she engaged in conversation with Miss Jones.

“Oh, my dear, how can I tell? Lovers, I suppose, or else they think they are clever, and then they talk rubbish.”

“Well, of course, Minora thinks she is clever.”

“I suppose she does. What does it matter what she thinks? Why does your governess look so gloomy? When I see her at luncheon I always imagine she must have just heard that somebody is dead. But she can’t hear that every day. What is the matter with her?”

“I don’t think she feels quite as proper as she looks,” I said doubtfully; I was forever trying to account for Miss Jones’s expression.

“But that must be rather nice,” said Irais. “It would be awful for her if she felt exactly the same as she looks.”

At that moment the door leading into the schoolroom opened softly, and the April baby, tired of playing, came in and sat down at my feet, leaving the door open; and this is what we heard Miss Jones saying﻿—

===Chinese===
「她们在谈什么呢？」昨天我问伊蕾斯，那时怎么也请不来米诺拉喝茶，她和琼斯小姐谈得太投入了。

「哦，亲爱的，我哪知道？多半是情人呗；要么就是她们自以为聪明，那就净说傻话。」

「嗯，当然啦，米诺拉就觉得自己聪明。」

「她大概是这么想的。她怎么想有什么要紧？你们那位家庭女教师怎么老拉着脸？午宴上我看见她，总疑心她准是刚听说谁死了。可她总不能天天都听说谁死啊。她到底怎么了？」

「我看她未必像她看上去那么规矩，」我迟疑地说；我一直在没法解释琼斯小姐的表情。

「可那样岂不挺好，」伊蕾斯说，「她要是里外一模一样，那才可怕呢。」

正在这时，通向儿童活动室的门轻轻开了，四月宝贝玩累了，走进来坐在我脚边，门没带上；于是我们听见琼斯小姐正说着——

===Original===
“Parents are seldom wise, and the strain the conscientious place upon themselves to appear so before their children and governess must be terrible. Nor are clergymen more pious than other men, yet they have continually to pose before their flock as such. As for governesses, Miss Minora, I know what I am saying when I affirm that there is nothing more intolerable than to have to be polite, and even humble, to persons whose weaknesses and follies are glaringly apparent in every word they utter, and to be forced by the presence of children and employers to a dignity of manner in no way corresponding to one’s feelings. The grave father of a family, who was probably one of the least respectable of bachelors, is an interesting study at his own table, where he is constrained to assume airs of infallibility merely because his children are looking at him. The fact of his being a parent does not endow him with any supreme and sudden virtue; and I can assure you that among the eyes fixed upon him, not the least critical and amused are those of the humble person who fills the post of governess.”

“Oh, Miss Jones, how lovely!” we heard Minora say in accents of rapture, while we sat transfixed with horror at these sentiments. “Do you mind if I put that down in my book? You say it all so beautifully.”

“Without a few hours of relaxation,” continued Miss Jones, “of private indemnification for the toilsome virtues displayed in public, who could wade through days of correct behaviour? There would be no reaction, no room for better impulses, no place for repentance. Parents, priests, and governesses would be in the situation of a stout lady who never has a quiet moment in which she can take off her corsets.”

===Chinese===
